### Task 4: P-c, the openings table, reads and erasure (additive); the atomic append in F

**Files:** `crates/kernel/chio-kernel/src/receipt_store.rs` (trait), every impl of `chio_kernel::ReceiptStore` (the trait in `crates/kernel/chio-kernel/src/receipt_store.rs`; 62 on `origin/main` `f447e922a9` by `git grep -nE 'impl(<[^>]*>)? +([A-Za-z_:]*::)?ReceiptStore +for ' -- . ':!docs' ':!crates/trust/chio-federation'`, 15 outside test directories: `SqliteReceiptStore`, `SqliteAdmissionOperationStore`, `RemoteReceiptStore`, `RemoteAdmissionAuthority` and the kernel, tower and broker test doubles; `chio_federation::bilateral_verifier::ReceiptStore` is a separate verifier lookup trait with only `resolve`, so neither it nor its `InMemoryReceiptStore` is in scope), `crates/platform/chio-store-sqlite/src/receipt_store/{bootstrap/open.rs,append.rs,openings.rs (new)}`, `crates/platform/chio-control-plane/src/trust_control/service_runtime/remote_stores.rs`, the trust-control receipt routes, `crates/platform/chio-control-plane/src/trust_control/cluster/deltas.rs` (`sync_peer_tool_receipts`), the store's v1 catalog validation.

**Split between P-c and F (P3-2).** P-c adds the table, the read and erase methods on every impl, the trust-control read and erase routes, tombstone replication and catalog validation. It adds no append argument and no transitional path. F changes `append_chio_receipt` to take `&SignedReceipt` and writes the openings, in one change, so no dual append exists on `main` at any point.

**Design (Root-2 conditions 1 to 3 and 6; lead r2 P1-B, P1-C, P2-B, c2):**
- Openings are written inside the store-owned `ReceiptStore::append_chio_receipt` transaction, under #1708's existing fence (rank 1) and receipts (rank 2) guards. No lock, rank or callback is added, and the projection guard (rank 3) is unchanged. A successful append acknowledges the commit of the receipt and its openings together; an error does not prove non-commit, so the idempotent retry below covers the unknown-ACK case. The replay path (`receipts(true)`) reads `opening_status` and `read_openings` only outside the publication guards. There is no separate opening write, so spec 8.4's "opening marked lost" state cannot arise on a hosted path (OQ2).
- Crash before commit: neither row exists and today's append-failure path applies. Crash after commit: both rows exist. A non-admission publication path that loses its in-memory `SignedReceipt` before the ACK either committed both rows or loses the receipt exactly as today; nothing re-signs.
- Remote trust-control (`--control-url`, used by `chio mcp serve`, `chio run` and `serve-http`): F's remote append request carries the receipt, `SignedReceipt.tenant` and the complete `SignedReceipt.openings` (the parameter and content salts and every `metadata.*` salt with its canonical value, projection fields included), and trust-control writes the receipt row, its `receipt_openings` rows and its `receipt_metadata_values` rows in one transaction on its own SQLite store. The client never stores openings locally. P-c adds two routes: the public `GET /v1/receipts/{id}/openings/status`, which returns only `{"state": "present" | "erased" | "unknown"}` and never a salt, and `POST /v1/receipts/{id}/openings/erase`; plus the internal opening read below.
- Cluster replication (lead r2 P1-B option (a); transport per lead r3 P1-6):
  - **Receipt deltas carry their openings (Root-2 r5, option (a)).** Each record of the receipt `seq` delta (`/v1/internal/receipts/tools/delta`, `list_tool_receipts_after_seq_with_context`) carries the receipt, its unsigned `tenant` and its complete current opening state as the source holds it when it serves the page: present salts with their `receipt_metadata_values` rows, or, if the source already erased them, the erased rows, the receipt's `receipt_opening_erasures` rows with their origin pairs and its `receipt_erasure_facts` row. The receiver imports each record through the same merge as a snapshot, writing the receipt row, its opening and values rows, any erasure rows, the erasure fact and the `peer_tool_seq` advance in one transaction, so no receipt becomes visible without its opening state, and a crash leaves either the whole record or nothing. A separate openings stream carries only later state changes. Each erase appends one `receipt_opening_erasures` row (DDL below) in the erasing transaction: a local `openings_seq` from `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT`, so a value is never reused after retention deletes rows, the receipt id, the origin pair and `erased_at`. An erasure imported from a peer, a receipt delta or a snapshot gets its own local `openings_seq` the same way, so every node serves every erasure it holds. An erasure conflict reaches peers on this stream as well: the node re-originates the erasure under its own origin (Merge), so the stream carries it even when the node does not hold the receipt. `/v1/internal/receipts/openings/delta` returns erasure rows after the requested `openings_seq`, in `openings_seq` order. Each node keeps one durable cursor per source in `receipt_openings_peer_cursors` (`partition.rs` holds `peer_openings_seq` beside `peer_tool_seq` as the in-memory copy, loaded from that table at startup), and `sync_peer_receipt_openings` applies each page and advances that cursor in one transaction. A source that restarts serves the same erasure rows under the same `openings_seq` values, so a peer whose cursor is below an erase still receives it. The hosted append and every replicated import are therefore atomic per receipt; erasure reaches peers within one openings-sync interval (PROTOCOL 6 states this, lead r5 P3-3).
  - **Merge.** A tombstone always wins. Every applied erasure, normal or conflicted, writes the receipt's `receipt_erasure_facts` row (DDL below) in its transaction, including an erasure for a receipt id the node does not hold. A replicated erasure for an unknown receipt id is stored as its `receipt_opening_erasures` row and its erasure fact (no table has a foreign key, and the replication path does not apply the client path's `ReceiptNotPublished` refusal). A later receipt, present-salt row or metadata value for that id from any stream finds the erasure fact and is written erased, never a salt or a value, and so is any `salt_ref` the receipt did not have before, such as a new metadata field. A second fact for a receipt keeps the first row: a later normal erasure of the same receipt by another origin leaves it unchanged, and a conflicted erasure sets `conflicted = 1`, which never returns to 0. An erasure whose origin pair is already stored is a no-op only when its `receipt_id` and `erased_at` equal the stored row's. A pair at or below that origin's `applied_through` whose log row was collected is a no-op only when the receipt's erasure fact names the same origin pair and `erased_at`. When the fact names a different origin, because another origin erased the receipt first, an after-collection re-send from the second origin is a conflict (case (ii) below) and freezes that origin until an operator resolves it. Any other erasure whose origin pair is stored, or is at or below `applied_through`, is an erasure conflict, never a new erasure. In every conflict the incoming event's tombstone for its own `receipt_id` is applied, because a tombstone always wins: that receipt's `receipt_openings` and `receipt_metadata_values` rows are erased locally and its erasure fact is written with `conflicted = 1`, so a conflict never leaves salts an origin asked to erase. No `receipt_opening_erasures` row is written for that origin pair, and a stored row stays as it is. In the same transaction the node re-originates the erasure: it appends a normal `receipt_opening_erasures` row for the incoming `receipt_id` under its own `origin_store_id`, with its next gap-free `origin_openings_seq` (its own `applied_through` plus one, advanced in that transaction, as for a local erase) and the fact's `erased_at`. Re-origination applies to every conflict, case (i) or (ii), whether or not the node holds the receipt. Peers receive the re-originated erasure on the ordinary openings stream within one sync interval, tombstone the receipt and write their own fact under the ordinary dedup, so a peer that already holds a fact for the receipt keeps its first fact. The row commits with the fact, so it survives a crash or restart before delivery. The conflicting foreign pair stays quarantined and is never written as a log row. A `replication_quarantine_erasures` row (DDL below, the erasure-conflict part of the replication quarantine) records the origin pair, the stored `receipt_id` and `erased_at` (NULL when the log row was collected), the incoming `receipt_id` and `erased_at`, the source node id, reason `erasure_event_conflict` and the hold, `below_watermark` or `origin_frozen`; the page cursor still advances. (i) When the conflicting `origin_openings_seq` is above that origin's `applied_through`, the hold is `below_watermark`: `applied_through` stays below the conflicting sequence (Retention), so the conflict is never acknowledged as applied and the origin's GC horizon holds behind it. (ii) When it is at or below `applied_through`, whether the stored row exists or was collected, the hold is `origin_frozen`, a durable conflicted state of that origin: `applied_through` and `gc_horizon` are never lowered, and they freeze at their current values, so that origin's watermark does not advance and none of its erasure rows is collected. The node reports the frozen origin in its signed cluster status, and every peer that sees the report freezes GC for that origin too. A node's own sequence is authoritative, and its erasure is never blocked: an incoming erasure whose pair names the node's own `origin_store_id` and differs from the node's own log row for that sequence, or names a sequence above the node's own `applied_through` (an event the node never emitted), is an own-origin conflict. The node records it with reason `erasure_event_conflict_own_origin` and hold `own_origin`, applies its tombstone and re-originates it as above; its own allocation keeps advancing for local and re-originated erasures and never moves to or past the incoming sequence because of it, and only the GC horizon of its own origin freezes until an operator resolves the quarantine row. An origin's hold is derived only from its unresolved `replication_quarantine_erasures` rows (`resolved_at` NULL): any `origin_frozen` row freezes its `applied_through` and `gc_horizon`, any `own_origin` row freezes its `gc_horizon` while the named store keeps allocating its own sequences, and its `below_watermark` rows keep `applied_through` below the lowest of their sequences. Every hold lasts until an operator resolves its row by setting `resolved_at`; nothing resolves it automatically, and a resolved row is kept. A present row for an id already present with identical salts is a no-op; with different salts it is recorded in a per-row `replication_quarantine` table (receipt id, salt_ref, origin node, reason) and skipped, and the page still advances, so a conflict never wedges the loop. The client retry path keeps its `ConflictingOpenings` refusal; the replication path never returns it.
  - **Snapshots.** `cluster/snapshots.rs` exports `receipt_openings` rows (present and tombstone), `receipt_metadata_values` rows, `receipt_opening_erasures` rows, `receipt_erasure_facts` rows, the unresolved `replication_quarantine_erasures` rows and `receipt_openings_watermarks` beside the tool receipts (`snapshots.rs:166-171`) and imports them at `snapshots.rs:365-368` through the same merge as ordinary deltas, in the same transaction as the receipt rows, so a node rebuilt from a stale snapshot applies every tombstone it carries and the next openings-stream sync delivers the rest. The conflict rows travel with the watermarks under the same snapshot signature, and the import writes them in the same transaction as the watermarks, so a node rebuilt from a snapshot derives every origin's hold (Merge) before it advances any watermark or collects any row: a frozen origin stays frozen and a held one stays held until operator resolution. The import merges each conflict row in that transaction, before it advances any watermark or collects any row, and never lowers a watermark or horizon. (a) A row whose origin is the importing store's own store id is normalized to own-origin semantics: reason `erasure_event_conflict_own_origin` and hold `own_origin`, so it freezes only the GC horizon of the importer's own origin and the importer's own allocation keeps advancing; an imported `below_watermark` or `origin_frozen` row naming the importer never freezes its `applied_through`, and an existing `own_origin` row is kept as is. (b) Hold strength is ordered `below_watermark` < `origin_frozen`. A row new to the importer is written with its hold, raised to `origin_frozen` when it is `below_watermark` and its sequence is at or below the importer's resulting `applied_through` for that origin. (c) An existing unresolved row for the same event (the same origin pair and incoming `receipt_id` and `erased_at`) takes the stronger of its own hold, the imported hold and the hold rule (b) gives; when that raises it, the import updates `hold` from `below_watermark` to `origin_frozen` in the same transaction. Its `source_node_id` and `recorded_at` stay local. An existing row that the operator resolved stays resolved and is not raised: operator resolution is per node, so the operator runbook resolves the same conflict on every node that holds it, and a peer's unresolved copy never re-opens a locally resolved row. (d) Any other difference with an existing row for the same event, a different `reason` (own-origin against a plain hold) or a different stored event where both rows carry one, refuses the whole snapshot with `ReceiptStoreError::SnapshotErasureConflictMismatch { origin_store_id, origin_openings_seq }`, and the transaction applies nothing. A stored event that one side carries and the other records as collected (NULL) is not a difference. No receipt-only import exists.
  - **Store identity (owner decision).** Every receipt store mints a random 128-bit store id from the `Rng` seam (`OsEntropyRng`) when it is created and writes it once to `receipt_store_identity`, in the creating transaction together with its own `receipt_openings_watermarks` row `(store_id, 0, 0)`; a failed draw refuses creation. The store id is the store's erasure origin for its whole life and is separate from the P-f cluster `node_id`: a store with no cluster configuration erases under its store id, and when it later joins a cluster its existing erasure rows keep that origin and peers track it as one more origin store with its own watermark and GC horizon. Open-time catalog validation refuses, with an exact error and no mutation, a missing identity row or a malformed id (`ReceiptStoreError::StoreIdMissing`) and an identity row with no matching own watermark row, or a layout without any of the three immutability triggers `receipt_store_identity_no_replace`, `receipt_store_identity_no_update` and `receipt_store_identity_no_delete` (`ReceiptStoreError::StoreIdChanged`).
  - **Retention (lead r5 P3-4; Root-2 r6).** Tombstones are not pruned with receipts, and no node ever compares bare local sequence numbers, because a replicated receipt gets a new local `seq` on each node (`receipt_store/append.rs:45-49`) and the same receipt can be `seq` 5 on one node and 100 on another. Every erasure row instead carries its origin: `(origin_store_id, origin_openings_seq)`, assigned once by the store that performed the erase and replicated unchanged. `origin_store_id` is the erasing store's store id (below); `origin_openings_seq` is that store's own `applied_through` plus one, advanced in the erasing transaction, so each origin's sequence is gap-free and independent of local `openings_seq`. Each node keeps, per origin, a contiguous applied watermark `openings_applied_through[origin]` (`receipt_openings_watermarks.applied_through`): the highest origin sequence below which it has applied every erasure of that origin, advanced only over a gap-free prefix that holds no unresolved `below_watermark` conflict for that origin, and not at all while the origin is frozen (Merge). An erasure that arrives out of order through a snapshot or a third node is applied at once; the per-origin pending set is that origin's erasure rows above `applied_through`, and the watermark advances when the prefix closes. Nodes report these per-origin watermarks in their signed cluster status, and each node stores every member's last report in `receipt_openings_member_watermarks`. A node's GC horizon for an origin is the minimum of the watermarks every configured cluster member reports for that origin; configured members include offline ones, so an unreachable member counts with its last reported watermark or, if it has never reported, holds the horizon at zero. An erasure row is deleted only when its `origin_openings_seq` is at or below that horizon and its receipt has been pruned locally (the receipt's erased opening and values rows go with the receipt); a held watermark holds the horizon, so no erasure row of that origin at or above the conflicting sequence is collected while the conflict is unresolved; a frozen origin's `gc_horizon` does not advance and none of its erasure rows is collected, on this node and on every peer that sees the frozen origin in a signed cluster status; GC never deletes a `receipt_erasure_facts` row, because the receipt can still arrive from a snapshot, a receipt delta or a client retry after its log row is collected; a peer that does not report keeps the horizon from advancing, which is safe and bounded only by that peer's return. Anti-resurrection evidence survives GC: each node stores its per-origin GC horizon durably (`receipt_openings_watermarks.gc_horizon`, never above `applied_through`), every replication source states its per-origin watermarks with each delta page and snapshot, and an import from a source whose watermark for any origin is below the importer's horizon is refused with `StaleReplicationSource`. Each stated watermark is bound to the exact payload it accompanies: it is covered by the same signature as that snapshot or delta page, and an import that applies a payload records the stated watermarks in the same transaction. The GC horizon is preserved durably across restarts and is carried in snapshots, so a node built from a snapshot imports the horizon with it and starts no lower than its source. A peer rejoining after pruning therefore cannot replay pre-erase state and must rebuild from a snapshot whose watermarks are at or above the horizon.
  - **Credential.** Peers authenticate with P-f peer credentials (one Ed25519 key per node, pinned in the cluster configuration beside `peer_urls`, scope `cluster-openings:replicate`) on the internal listener; `validate_cluster_peer_auth` checks the signature against the pinned key of the claimed `node_id`. The signed payload (`chio.cluster.peer-signature.v1`, #1729) binds the scheme, the sender node id, the intended receiver node id, the deployment id, the method, the path, the raw query (which carries the cursor), `issued_at` within the freshness window, the cluster term, the SHA-256 of the request body and a single-use nonce. The receiver refuses a transcript whose receiver node id or deployment id differs from its own configuration, so a request signed for one receiver does not authenticate at another receiver that pins the same sender key; nonce tracking alone gives no such binding. It records each nonce for the freshness window and refuses a replay. Tests: `peer_request_for_another_receiver_refuses` (cross-receiver negative control), `peer_request_for_another_deployment_refuses`, `peer_nonce_replay_refuses`, `peer_body_digest_mismatch_refuses`. The shared `service_token` HMAC no longer authenticates any cluster route.
  - **Tests:** `receipt_delta_imports_receipt_and_openings_atomically`, `receipt_delta_first_then_openings_tombstone`, `openings_tombstone_first_then_receipt_delta`, `tombstone_first_for_unknown_receipt_then_delta_writes_no_salt`, `crash_between_receipt_delta_and_openings_stream_leaves_receipt_with_full_opening_state`, `crash_inside_receipt_delta_import_leaves_nothing`, `no_receipt_visible_without_opening_state`, `erase_emits_new_openings_seq`, `erase_replicates_tombstone_to_peers`, `peer_delta_with_salts_does_not_resurrect_tombstone`, `snapshot_import_applies_tombstones`, `stale_snapshot_rebuild_does_not_resurrect_salts`, `conflicting_replicated_salts_quarantined_and_sync_advances`, `tombstone_retained_until_every_peer_acks_and_prunes`, `two_origins_with_overlapping_sequence_numbers_prune_independently`, `same_receipt_with_different_local_seq_on_two_nodes_prunes_by_origin`, `out_of_order_tombstones_hold_watermark_until_prefix_closes`, `rejoining_peer_after_gc_refused_until_fresh_snapshot`, `stale_snapshot_replay_after_gc_refused`, `client_credential_cannot_read_replicated_salts`, `peer_signature_covers_cursor_and_term`, `peer_offline_during_erase_erases_on_next_sync`.
- Durable admission: when a terminal receipt is durably admitted before publication, the admission row holds the signed receipt, its `tenant` and its complete `SignedReceipt.openings` (salts and metadata values), so recovery republishes the exact receipt, salts and values (Root-2 condition 3). The admission row's salts and values are cleared in the transaction that records the publication acknowledgement, because recovery needs them only before it; an `erase_openings` call before that point refuses with `ReceiptStoreError::ReceiptNotPublished`.
- Idempotency: an append of a receipt id that exists with byte-identical receipt, salts and metadata values is a no-op success; with different bytes, salts or values it refuses with `ReceiptStoreError::ConflictingOpenings`. A retry resends the same request, held in memory until ACK or recovered from the admission row. Nothing re-signs.
- Erasure: `erase_openings(receipt_id, erased_at)` replaces the receipt's `receipt_openings` rows and `receipt_metadata_values` rows with tombstones, appends its `receipt_opening_erasures` row under the store's own origin, writes the receipt's `receipt_erasure_facts` row and advances that origin's `applied_through`, in one transaction. A re-originated conflict erasure (Merge) appends its row under the store's own origin the same way. Every write of opening or values rows (the local and remote append, admission recovery, receipt delta and snapshot import, and a later write of a `salt_ref` the receipt did not have) first reads `receipt_erasure_facts`, and a receipt with a fact gets every row written erased. A missing row reads as `OpeningState::Unknown`, never as erased or lost.
- **Re-runs never need an opening.** Openings serve first-execution checks only: the producing process's check before its receipt is published, and a new operation's first check of an earlier receipt (kind E-k), which refuses on erasure. No check that can run again during replay, recovery, re-projection or ledger replay requires an opening. A re-run that compares receipts of the same operation compares the commitment values its component recorded at first execution (kind A); a re-run that needs plaintext reads it from the component's own private state, persisted at first execution, never from receipt openings. Erasure therefore never fails a re-run: it fails only a first-execution check and a reader of a published receipt's plaintext (`OpeningUnavailable`, `ReceiptMetadataUnavailable`). Task 7 lists every re-run site and the state it reads.
- Internal opening read (Root-2 r3 and r4; lead r4-delta e2 (a) and e3): `POST /v1/internal/receipts/openings/read` on trust-control's internal listener (P-f), with body `{"receipt_id", "salt_refs": [..]}` and nothing else. Authorization comes only from the authenticated P-f caller credential, never from the request:
  - **Scope.** The credential must carry `receipt-openings:read`. The P-f registry grants it only to the kernel-hosting processes of one deployment (`serve-http`, `chio mcp serve` and `chio run` with `--control-url`) and to the control plane's security and settlement services. Evaluate, caller, admin-UI and tenant-read credentials do not carry it.
  - **Deployment.** The credential names one deployment; the receipt's `kernel_key` must be one of that deployment's kernel keys, or the read is refused.
  - **Tenant.** The credential's tenant authority is `Deployment` or `Tenant(t)`. For `Tenant(t)`, trust-control derives the tenant from the credential and checks the receipt's signed form itself: a signed `tenant_id` must equal `t`; a signed `tenant_binding` must pass `TenantIndexKeySet::verify(t, &binding)`, computed server-side, which selects the generation by the binding's `key_id` (active or retired), derives under that generation's `tenant-binding` subkey and compares in constant time. The check never compares against `bind(t)`, which always derives under the active generation, and an unknown `key_id` refuses (`TenantBindingError::TenantIndexKeyUnknown`). The public status route applies the same tenant check to a `Tenant(t)` caller. A binding copied from the receipt into the request is never an input, so it proves nothing (test `internal_read_refuses_binding_copied_from_receipt`). A `Deployment` credential is entitled to either form for receipts its deployment signed. A receipt carrying both forms is malformed and refused. A receipt carrying neither is untenanted and is returned only to a `Deployment` credential.
  - **Response.** `{"state":"present","openings":[..]}` (only the requested `salt_ref`s), `{"state":"erased","erased_at":..}` or `{"state":"unknown"}`, with `Cache-Control: no-store`. Every read is written to the trust-control audit log with the credential id, receipt id and `salt_ref`s, never the salts.
  - **Callers.** `RemoteReceiptStore::read_openings`; the mcp-remote output path, E-k and A/E under `--control-url`, and the settle and web3 anchor-proof producer.
- Stores without openings: every impl of `chio_kernel::ReceiptStore` implements `opening_status`, `read_openings` and `erase_openings` explicitly (no default method). A store that keeps no openings (test doubles, `InMemoryReceiptStore`) returns `Unknown` from `opening_status` and `read_openings` and `Ok(())` from `erase_openings`, because no salt exists to erase.

**Salt copies and erasure (P1-C).** Every place a salt is stored, and whether `erase_openings` reaches it:

| Copy | Erasure |
|---|---|
| `receipt_openings` rows (local SQLite, trust-control) | tombstoned; the receipt's `receipt_erasure_facts` row keeps every later write for it erased |
| cluster peers' `receipt_openings` | the erase emits a new `openings_seq`; peers pull the tombstone on the openings stream or through snapshot import and record the receipt's erasure fact, and a conflicted erasure reaches them through its re-originated row; tombstones outlive receipt retention and erasure facts are never deleted; a peer offline during the erase erases on its next sync |
| admission row (pre-publication only) | cleared at the publication acknowledgement; test `admission_row_salts_cleared_at_publication`; erase before publication refuses |
| in-memory `SignedReceipt`, retry buffers | zeroized on drop (`Salt: ZeroizeOnDrop`) |
| `chio run` disclosure documents written beside the evidence export | not reached: the export is the operator's own file once written |
| kind E carriers (commerce authority receipt artifacts, enterprise export events, agent-web envelopes, settle and web3 anchor proofs, Mercury and attest-loopback packages, finding challenge evidence, finding bundle runtime-assurance openings, native launch evidence (`NativeLaunchEvidence`, `NativeLaunchObservation`)) | not reached: each carrier already holds the committed preimage in plaintext, so its salt reveals nothing the carrier does not hold |
| relay-host copies: the salt-only `chio.receipt-disclosure.v1` that the browser WASM, TS `workers`, `edge` and `deno`, mobile and C ABI relays return with each receipt (Task 9) | not reached: the host holds them, and store erasure has no path to them |
| caller copies from in-process signers: the portable signer's `SignedReceipt`, the BBS selective-disclosure caller, and the runtime harness, conformance native suite and attest loopback callers (producer table) | not reached: once returned they are the caller's |
| `/v1/evaluate` responses | none in M1 (open question 10) |

PROTOCOL 6 (F) states that erasure makes the stored openings and metadata values permanently unavailable and does not reach openings already delivered in exports, carriers, relay hosts or callers. Test `erase_clears_store_and_admission_copies`.

**Interfaces:**

```rust
// Produces (P-c): the opening-state types the trait below and every opening reader use.
/// Salts read back from a store for one receipt, keyed by `salt_ref`, holding only the refs
/// the read requested (`params`, `content`, `metadata.<block>.<field>`, `anchor.*`). Zeroized on
/// drop; Debug prints the receipt id and the `salt_ref`s only. `ReceiptOpenings` (Task 6) is the
/// complete set a signed receipt carries to its append.
pub struct OpenedSalts { pub receipt_id: String, pub salts: BTreeMap<SaltRef, Salt> }
pub enum OpeningStateKind { Erased, Unknown }
/// The persisted state of one receipt's openings.
pub enum OpeningState { Present(OpenedSalts), Erased { erased_at: u64 }, Unknown }
/// Salt-free status, the only form the public status route and logs carry.
pub enum OpeningStatus { Present, Erased { erased_at: u64 }, Unknown }
/// What a reader may see: the `salt_ref`s it needs and the tenant binding it is scoped to.
pub struct OpeningReadScope { pub salt_refs: Vec<SaltRef>, pub tenant_binding: Option<TenantBinding> }
pub struct OpeningUnavailable { pub receipt_id: String, pub state: OpeningStateKind }

pub trait ReceiptStore {
    /// F: writes the receipt row, one `receipt_openings` row per salt in `signed.openings`, one
    /// `receipt_metadata_values` row per committed metadata value and the tenant column from
    /// `signed.tenant`, in one store transaction. Replaces the current receipt argument of
    /// `append_chio_receipt` and every `append_chio_receipt_*` variant.
    fn append_chio_receipt(&self, signed: &SignedReceipt) -> Result<(), ReceiptStoreError>;
    /// P-c. Required on every impl. Never returns salts.
    fn opening_status(&self, receipt_id: &str) -> Result<OpeningStatus, ReceiptStoreError>;
    /// P-c. Required on every impl. Returns salts only for the requested refs and only within
    /// `scope` (`OpeningState::Present(OpenedSalts)`, above); a remote store calls the internal
    /// opening read. Never returns metadata values.
    fn read_openings(&self, receipt_id: &str, scope: &OpeningReadScope) -> Result<OpeningState, ReceiptStoreError>;
    /// P-c. Required on every impl.
    fn erase_openings(&self, receipt_id: &str, erased_at: u64) -> Result<(), ReceiptStoreError>;
}
```

DDL (fresh-only v1; catalog validation refuses any other layout with an exact error and mutates nothing, Root-2 condition 6; the layout includes the `sqlite_sequence` row that `AUTOINCREMENT` creates):

```sql
CREATE TABLE receipt_openings (
  receipt_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(receipt_id) = 'text' AND instr(receipt_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(receipt_id) = 64 AND receipt_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  salt_ref TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(salt_ref) = 'text' AND instr(salt_ref, char(0)) = 0 AND length(salt_ref) BETWEEN 1 AND 64 AND salt_ref GLOB '[a-z]*' AND salt_ref NOT GLOB '*[^a-z0-9_.]*' AND salt_ref NOT GLOB '*..*' AND salt_ref NOT GLOB '*.'),
  state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(state) = 'text' AND instr(state, char(0)) = 0 AND state IN ('present', 'erased')),
  salt BLOB,
  state_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(state_at) = 'integer' AND state_at >= 0),
  CHECK ((state = 'present' AND typeof(salt) = 'blob' AND length(salt) = 32) OR (state = 'erased' AND salt IS NULL)),
  PRIMARY KEY (receipt_id, salt_ref)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE TABLE receipt_store_identity (
  singleton INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK (singleton = 1),
  store_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(store_id) = 'text' AND instr(store_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(store_id) = 32 AND store_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*')
);
CREATE TRIGGER receipt_store_identity_no_replace BEFORE INSERT ON receipt_store_identity WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM receipt_store_identity) BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'receipt store id is immutable'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER receipt_store_identity_no_update BEFORE UPDATE ON receipt_store_identity BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'receipt store id is immutable'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER receipt_store_identity_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON receipt_store_identity BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'receipt store id is immutable'); END;
CREATE TABLE receipt_opening_erasures (
  openings_seq INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  receipt_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(receipt_id) = 'text' AND instr(receipt_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(receipt_id) = 64 AND receipt_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  origin_store_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_store_id) = 'text' AND instr(origin_store_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(origin_store_id) = 32 AND origin_store_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  origin_openings_seq INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_openings_seq) = 'integer' AND origin_openings_seq >= 1),
  erased_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(erased_at) = 'integer' AND erased_at >= 0),
  UNIQUE (origin_store_id, origin_openings_seq)
);
CREATE INDEX receipt_opening_erasures_by_receipt ON receipt_opening_erasures (receipt_id);
CREATE TABLE receipt_erasure_facts (
  receipt_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(receipt_id) = 'text' AND instr(receipt_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(receipt_id) = 64 AND receipt_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  origin_store_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_store_id) = 'text' AND instr(origin_store_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(origin_store_id) = 32 AND origin_store_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  origin_openings_seq INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_openings_seq) = 'integer' AND origin_openings_seq >= 1),
  erased_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(erased_at) = 'integer' AND erased_at >= 0),
  recorded_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(recorded_at) = 'integer' AND recorded_at >= 0),
  conflicted INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(conflicted) = 'integer' AND conflicted IN (0, 1)),
  PRIMARY KEY (receipt_id)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE TRIGGER receipt_erasure_facts_no_replace BEFORE INSERT ON receipt_erasure_facts WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM receipt_erasure_facts WHERE receipt_id = NEW.receipt_id) BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'receipt erasure fact is permanent'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER receipt_erasure_facts_only_marks_conflict BEFORE UPDATE ON receipt_erasure_facts WHEN NEW.receipt_id IS NOT OLD.receipt_id OR NEW.origin_store_id IS NOT OLD.origin_store_id OR NEW.origin_openings_seq IS NOT OLD.origin_openings_seq OR NEW.erased_at IS NOT OLD.erased_at OR NEW.recorded_at IS NOT OLD.recorded_at OR NEW.conflicted < OLD.conflicted BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'receipt erasure fact is permanent'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER receipt_erasure_facts_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON receipt_erasure_facts BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'receipt erasure fact is permanent'); END;
CREATE TABLE replication_quarantine_erasures (
  quarantine_id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT,
  origin_store_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_store_id) = 'text' AND instr(origin_store_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(origin_store_id) = 32 AND origin_store_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  origin_openings_seq INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_openings_seq) = 'integer' AND origin_openings_seq >= 1),
  reason TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(reason) = 'text' AND reason IN ('erasure_event_conflict', 'erasure_event_conflict_own_origin')),
  hold TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(hold) = 'text' AND hold IN ('below_watermark', 'origin_frozen', 'own_origin')),
  stored_receipt_id TEXT CHECK (stored_receipt_id IS NULL OR (typeof(stored_receipt_id) = 'text' AND instr(stored_receipt_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(stored_receipt_id) = 64 AND stored_receipt_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*')),
  stored_erased_at INTEGER CHECK (stored_erased_at IS NULL OR (typeof(stored_erased_at) = 'integer' AND stored_erased_at >= 0)),
  incoming_receipt_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(incoming_receipt_id) = 'text' AND instr(incoming_receipt_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(incoming_receipt_id) = 64 AND incoming_receipt_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  incoming_erased_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(incoming_erased_at) = 'integer' AND incoming_erased_at >= 0),
  source_node_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(source_node_id) = 'text' AND instr(source_node_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(source_node_id) BETWEEN 1 AND 2048 AND octet_length(source_node_id) <= 8192),
  recorded_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(recorded_at) = 'integer' AND recorded_at >= 0),
  resolved_at INTEGER CHECK (resolved_at IS NULL OR (typeof(resolved_at) = 'integer' AND resolved_at >= recorded_at)),
  CHECK ((hold = 'own_origin') = (reason = 'erasure_event_conflict_own_origin')),
  CHECK ((stored_receipt_id IS NULL) = (stored_erased_at IS NULL)),
  UNIQUE (origin_store_id, origin_openings_seq, incoming_receipt_id, incoming_erased_at)
);
CREATE INDEX replication_quarantine_erasures_unresolved ON replication_quarantine_erasures (origin_store_id, hold) WHERE resolved_at IS NULL;
CREATE TRIGGER replication_quarantine_erasures_no_replace BEFORE INSERT ON replication_quarantine_erasures WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM replication_quarantine_erasures WHERE quarantine_id = NEW.quarantine_id OR (origin_store_id = NEW.origin_store_id AND origin_openings_seq = NEW.origin_openings_seq AND incoming_receipt_id = NEW.incoming_receipt_id AND incoming_erased_at = NEW.incoming_erased_at)) BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'erasure conflict record is permanent'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER replication_quarantine_erasures_own_store_is_own_origin BEFORE INSERT ON replication_quarantine_erasures WHEN NEW.hold <> 'own_origin' AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM receipt_store_identity WHERE store_id = NEW.origin_store_id) BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'a conflict naming this store has hold own_origin'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER replication_quarantine_erasures_monotone BEFORE UPDATE ON replication_quarantine_erasures WHEN OLD.resolved_at IS NOT NULL OR NEW.quarantine_id IS NOT OLD.quarantine_id OR NEW.origin_store_id IS NOT OLD.origin_store_id OR NEW.origin_openings_seq IS NOT OLD.origin_openings_seq OR NEW.reason IS NOT OLD.reason OR NEW.stored_receipt_id IS NOT OLD.stored_receipt_id OR NEW.stored_erased_at IS NOT OLD.stored_erased_at OR NEW.incoming_receipt_id IS NOT OLD.incoming_receipt_id OR NEW.incoming_erased_at IS NOT OLD.incoming_erased_at OR NEW.source_node_id IS NOT OLD.source_node_id OR NEW.recorded_at IS NOT OLD.recorded_at OR NOT ((OLD.hold = 'below_watermark' AND NEW.hold = 'origin_frozen' AND NEW.resolved_at IS NULL) OR (NEW.hold IS OLD.hold AND NEW.resolved_at IS NOT NULL)) BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'erasure conflict record is permanent'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER replication_quarantine_erasures_no_delete BEFORE DELETE ON replication_quarantine_erasures BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'erasure conflict record is permanent'); END;
CREATE TABLE receipt_openings_watermarks (
  origin_store_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_store_id) = 'text' AND instr(origin_store_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(origin_store_id) = 32 AND origin_store_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  applied_through INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(applied_through) = 'integer' AND applied_through >= 0),
  gc_horizon INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(gc_horizon) = 'integer' AND gc_horizon >= 0),
  CHECK (gc_horizon <= applied_through),
  PRIMARY KEY (origin_store_id)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE TABLE receipt_openings_member_watermarks (
  member_node_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(member_node_id) = 'text' AND instr(member_node_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(member_node_id) BETWEEN 1 AND 2048 AND octet_length(member_node_id) <= 8192),
  origin_store_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(origin_store_id) = 'text' AND instr(origin_store_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(origin_store_id) = 32 AND origin_store_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  applied_through INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(applied_through) = 'integer' AND applied_through >= 0),
  PRIMARY KEY (member_node_id, origin_store_id)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE TABLE receipt_openings_peer_cursors (
  peer_node_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(peer_node_id) = 'text' AND instr(peer_node_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(peer_node_id) BETWEEN 1 AND 2048 AND octet_length(peer_node_id) <= 8192),
  after_openings_seq INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(after_openings_seq) = 'integer' AND after_openings_seq >= 0),
  PRIMARY KEY (peer_node_id)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
```

`receipt_openings` holds the current state of each salt. `receipt_store_identity` holds the store id: one row, 32 lowercase hex. Three triggers keep it immutable: `receipt_store_identity_no_replace` refuses any INSERT once the row exists, which covers INSERT OR REPLACE and REPLACE (SQLite fires no delete trigger for their implicit delete while `recursive_triggers` is off, and the receipt store's connections leave it off), and `receipt_store_identity_no_update` and `receipt_store_identity_no_delete` refuse UPDATE and DELETE. This is the no-replace pattern of `admission_operation_dpop_replay.sql`. `receipt_opening_erasures` is the erasure log the openings stream pages, one row per erase event (a receipt erased by two stores has two rows, one per origin). `receipt_erasure_facts` is the receipt-wide erasure fact: one row per erased receipt, written by every applied erasure, normal or conflicted, with the first erasure's origin pair and `erased_at`, the local `recorded_at` and `conflicted` (INTEGER 0 or 1). Its three triggers refuse a second INSERT (INSERT OR REPLACE and REPLACE included), any DELETE, and any UPDATE other than setting `conflicted` from 0 to 1, so a fact is never replaced, cleared or collected. `replication_quarantine_erasures` holds one row per conflicting erasure event (`UNIQUE` on the origin pair and the incoming `receipt_id` and `erased_at`): `reason` and `hold` (a CHECK ties hold `own_origin` to reason `erasure_event_conflict_own_origin`), the stored event (NULL when its log row was collected), the incoming event, the source node id, `recorded_at` and `resolved_at`. Its triggers refuse a replacing INSERT, an INSERT with a hold other than `own_origin` for a conflict whose origin is this store's own id (from `receipt_store_identity`), any DELETE, and any UPDATE other than the two allowed changes on an unresolved row: raising `hold` from `below_watermark` to `origin_frozen` with `resolved_at` left NULL, and setting `resolved_at` once with `hold` unchanged. A hold is therefore never weakened, a resolved row never changes, a resolution is never undone, and a plain hold can never name the store's own origin; the partial index serves the hold derivation over unresolved rows. `openings_seq` is local and ordered; `(origin_store_id, origin_openings_seq)` is global and unique. `receipt_openings_watermarks` holds, per origin store, the contiguous applied watermark and the durable GC horizon, and the table-level check keeps the horizon at or below the watermark. `receipt_openings_member_watermarks` holds each configured cluster member's last reported watermark per origin store, the input of the horizon. `receipt_openings_peer_cursors` holds the `openings_seq` this node has applied from each source. Origin columns hold store ids and decode through the Rust `StoreId` type (32 lowercase hex); member and peer columns hold P-f's normalized cluster `node_id` and decode through P-f's `normalize_cluster_url`. A row that fails its decoder refuses with `ReceiptStoreError::CorruptOpeningRow`.

Every text key column also carries `instr(col, char(0)) = 0`, because SQLite's `length()` and `GLOB` stop at an embedded NUL, so `'params' || char(0) || 'BAD'` would otherwise pass the grammar (Root-2 r3). Length checks count characters (`length(col)`), never encoded bytes: `length(col)` doubles under a UTF-16 database encoding, so a UTF-16 store could not write its own store id. Hex ids and `salt_ref` are ASCII by their GLOB grammar, so the character count is exact in every encoding. The free-text node ids carry at most 2048 characters plus an `octet_length(col) <= 8192` ceiling (four bytes per character), which also refuses a UTF-8 value stretched with continuation bytes. The state check has two explicit branches (Root-2 r2 request 1): present means `typeof(salt) = 'blob'` and `length(salt) = 32`; erased means `salt IS NULL`. The `salt` column has BLOB affinity, so SQLite performs no type conversion and a TEXT or INTEGER value reaches the check as itself. The `salt_ref` check is #1706's grammar `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*(\.[a-z0-9_]+)*$` with at most 64 characters, written as GLOB clauses: the first character is a lowercase letter, every character is in `[a-z0-9_.]`, no empty segment (`..`) and no trailing `.`. Delegation-context claim salts (`anchor.claims.*`) fit without a later table change. The same grammar is enforced at the Rust boundary: every write takes a `SaltRef` (decoded through #1706's checked constructor) and every read decodes the column through that constructor, refusing a row that fails it with `ReceiptStoreError::CorruptOpeningRow`. Writer custody and fencing follow the existing serving writer.

The DDL was checked with `/usr/bin/sqlite3` 3.51.0 (the Mac CLI; ws2 has no sqlite3 binary), not with cargo. The probe (`docs/superpowers/plans/m1-item2-census/ddl-probe/{ddl.sql,probe.py,probe-result.json}`, run as `python3 -I probe.py /usr/bin/sqlite3 ddl.sql`; `probe-result.json` sha256 `ae492067bda708189dcf4c57df49312374b67caa0978693bc5ba2532ad3d48f1`) runs 28 INSERT cases and 6 UPDATE cases against a seeded present row of `receipt_openings`, and 91 cases on the store identity, the erasure log, the erasure facts, the erasure conflicts and the watermark and cursor tables. The six INSERT OR REPLACE, REPLACE and second-INSERT cases on the store identity each set `recursive_triggers` (three off, three on), print the setting, and after the refusal assert that the identity row and both watermark rows are unchanged; the refused second-INSERT, INSERT OR REPLACE (`recursive_triggers` off and on), UPDATE and DELETE cases on the erasure facts assert that the stored fact is unchanged, and the refused duplicate, INSERT OR REPLACE (`recursive_triggers` off and on), hold UPDATE and DELETE cases on the erasure conflicts assert that the stored conflict row is unchanged. The escalation, downgrade and other-column UPDATE cases on the erasure conflicts each run with `recursive_triggers` off and on and assert the resulting hold. All 125 behaved as expected:

| Case | Expected | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| INSERT present 32-byte BLOB; erased NULL; present `anchor.claims.client_id` (controls) | accept | accept |
| INSERT erased with a 1-byte BLOB, a 32-byte BLOB or TEXT | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT present with 32-character TEXT, NULL, a 31- or 33-byte BLOB, or INTEGER | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT state `lost`; state `'erased' || char(0) || 'x'` | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT salt_ref uppercase, `..`, trailing `.`, leading digit, `-`, empty, 65 characters | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT salt_ref `'params' || char(0) || 'BAD'` and `'params' || char(0)` (Root-2 r3) | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT receipt_id uppercase hex; 63 characters | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT receipt_id 64 hex then `char(0) || 'BAD'` (Root-2 r3); 64 bytes with a NUL inside | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT state_at TEXT; negative | refuse | refuse |
| UPDATE to erased clearing the salt (control) | accept | accept |
| UPDATE to erased leaving the salt | refuse | refuse |
| UPDATE salt_ref, receipt_id or state to a value with an embedded NUL | refuse | refuse |
| UPDATE salt to 32-character TEXT | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT store identity (control) | accept | accept |
| INSERT a second identity row or a duplicate singleton; a store id that is uppercase, 31 or 33 characters, holds a NUL or is a URL | refuse | refuse |
| UPDATE or DELETE the store identity | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT OR REPLACE, REPLACE or a second plain INSERT of the store identity, each with `recursive_triggers` off and on | refuse; identity and watermark rows unchanged | refuse; identity and watermark rows unchanged |
| INSERT erasure fact with `conflicted` 0 or 1 (controls); UPDATE `conflicted` from 0 to 1 (control) | accept | accept |
| INSERT erasure fact with `conflicted` 2, -1, TEXT or NULL, an uppercase receipt id, a node URL origin, origin sequence 0, a negative `erased_at` or `recorded_at` | refuse | refuse |
| second INSERT of an erasure fact; INSERT OR REPLACE with `recursive_triggers` off and on; UPDATE of `conflicted` from 1 to 0, of the origin or of `erased_at`; DELETE | refuse; stored fact unchanged | refuse; stored fact unchanged |
| INSERT erasure conflict with hold `below_watermark`, `origin_frozen` after GC (stored event NULL) and `own_origin` (controls); UPDATE `resolved_at` from NULL (control) | accept | accept |
| INSERT erasure conflict with an unknown hold, hold `own_origin` with the plain reason or the own-origin reason with another hold, a stored receipt without its `erased_at`, a node URL origin, an uppercase incoming receipt, or `resolved_at` before `recorded_at`; UPDATE `resolved_at` back to NULL or a second time | refuse | refuse |
| second INSERT of the same conflicting event; INSERT OR REPLACE with `recursive_triggers` off and on; UPDATE of `hold`; DELETE | refuse; stored conflict row unchanged | refuse; stored conflict row unchanged |
| UPDATE `hold` from `below_watermark` to `origin_frozen` on an unresolved row, with `recursive_triggers` off and on | accept; hold `origin_frozen` | accept; hold `origin_frozen` |
| UPDATE `hold` from `origin_frozen` to `below_watermark`, or of `source_node_id`, each with `recursive_triggers` off and on; UPDATE to `own_origin`; escalation together with `resolved_at`; escalation of a resolved row; an UPDATE that changes nothing | refuse; stored conflict row unchanged | refuse; stored conflict row unchanged |
| INSERT a plain-hold conflict whose origin is the store's own id | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT a plain-hold conflict naming another store, and an `own_origin` conflict naming the store's own id, with the store identity present (controls) | accept | accept |
| INSERT erasure row (control); the same receipt from two origins | accept | accept |
| INSERT erasure row with a duplicate origin pair, origin sequence 0 or TEXT, an origin that is empty, holds a NUL, is a node URL or is uppercase, an uppercase receipt id, a negative `erased_at`, or a TEXT `openings_seq` | refuse | refuse |
| `openings_seq` after deleting the highest row (`AUTOINCREMENT`; the case fails unless the next value is 3) | not reused | not reused |
| INSERT watermark (control); member watermark (control); peer cursor (control) | accept | accept |
| INSERT watermark with the horizon above `applied_through`, a negative watermark, a TEXT horizon or a node URL origin; UPDATE the horizon past `applied_through` | refuse | refuse |
| INSERT member watermark negative or with a node URL origin; peer cursor negative or TEXT | refuse | refuse |

above it is collected), `out_of_order_erasure_removes_salts_before_watermark_advances`, `peer_cursor_advances_with_its_page`; `every_store_impl_answers_opening_status_and_read` (one case per `chio_kernel::ReceiptStore` impl outside test directories); `old_layout_refuses_without_mutation` (catalog validation of `receipt_openings`, `receipt_store_identity` and its triggers, `receipt_opening_erasures`, `receipt_erasure_facts` and its triggers, `replication_quarantine_erasures` with its index and triggers, and the watermark and cursor tables).

### Task 5: P-d, guard evidence declarations and the metadata key registry (declarations only)

**Files:** both `Guard` traits, every impl including test impls (111 at `fcc31d25fb`), `chio-wasm-guards/src/runtime/guard.rs`, `chio-external-guards/src/lib.rs` (`ExternalGuard`, `ScopedAsyncGuard`), `crates/core/chio-core-types/src/receipt/metadata.rs`, `crates/kernel/chio-kernel/src/receipt_support/receipt_metadata.rs`, `spec/PROTOCOL.md` 6.4.

**Interfaces:**

```rust
pub enum EvidenceDisclosure { /// `details` never carries argument or result values.
    Safe, Unsafe }
pub struct GuardEvidenceDeclaration { pub details: EvidenceDisclosure, pub details_required: bool }
// Required on both Guard traits and on ExternalGuard; no default method.
fn evidence_declaration(&self) -> GuardEvidenceDeclaration;
```

- `WasmGuard` declares `{ details: Unsafe, details_required: false }` for every module, because one impl covers modules whose output Chio cannot inspect. `ScopedAsyncGuard<E>` returns `E::evidence_declaration()`.
- Registration refuses `{ details: Unsafe, details_required: true }` (spec 8.4 "fails at load"). This refusal is live in P-d; no current guard declares it, which P-d's test pins.
- Operator impact (P3): a guard declared `Unsafe` loses its `details` text in receipts from F onward, for example the forbidden-path guard's matched path. The text still reaches the kernel's structured deny log and the caller's error response, which the PR body states.

Metadata key registry (ADR-0019 item 7, built here as that ADR schedules): a named-const registry in `receipt/metadata.rs`:

```rust
pub enum MetadataKeyOrigin { ChioInternal, CallerOrHook }
pub enum MetadataDisclosure {
    /// Carries no argument or result value. `reason` records the field audit.
    CommittedSafe,
    /// Carries an argument or result value; refused in signed metadata.
    Unsafe }
pub struct MetadataKeySpec { pub key: &'static str, pub origin: MetadataKeyOrigin, pub disclosure: MetadataDisclosure,
    pub schema: Option<&'static str>, pub reason: &'static str }
pub const RECEIPT_METADATA_KEYS: &[MetadataKeySpec];
pub fn metadata_key_spec(key: &str) -> Option<&'static MetadataKeySpec>;
```

Every Chio-internal key the Task 1 metadata census found is registered with its declaration, including the operational blocks admission, active response and settlement need (`admission_operation`, `budget_authority`, `financial`, `execution_nonce_id`, `delivery_contract`, `caller_delivery`, `lineage`, `parent_receipt_ids`, `route_selection`, `security_event_projection`, `receipt_context`, `chio_runtime`, `post_invocation`, `mercury`), each with a `reason` naming its field audit. No key is added for the parameter readers: `delivery_preparation.rs` reads the signed `cage_receipt.enforcement_record.state`, and `finding_purchase_routes.rs` reads the existing `finding_delivery` key (`FINDING_DELIVERY_METADATA_KEY`, `finding_id`, a public listing id). `delivery_contract` is written for every grant with `Constraint::OutputDigestSha256`, and its `observed_digest` is the unsalted digest of the actual output; F splits it: (i) for a finding-delivery grant the digests equal the finding's `payload_sha256`, which the listing already publishes, so they stay plaintext and are registered `CommittedSafe` with that reason (the finding verifier, challenge and recovery readers bind to them, kind F; open question 12); (ii) for every other grant `expected_digest` stays (the grant holder set it) and `observed_digest` becomes a `MetadataCommitment` under `salt_ref` `metadata.delivery_contract.observed_digest`. The kernel checks it at signing with the output in hand; the admission row records the raw `delivery_observed_digest` and the commitment value `receipt_delivery_observed_commitment`, and recovery and replay compare those recorded values with no opening (Task 4 re-run rule, Task 7). `security_event_projection` carries `SecurityEventBody` (`tenant_id`, `subject`, `source_receipt_id`, `producer_id`), which is also the receipt's content preimage; it gets the same treatment as the two blocks below: a `security_event` projection with `tenant_binding` (keyed, Task 5 tenant binding) in place of `tenant_id`, `subject` as a `MetadataCommitment` (`metadata.security_event.subject`), and the Chio receipt and producer ids kept; `event_consumer/verification.rs` moves from `projection.body == *body` to `matches_with` over a verify-mode `MetadataCommitter` on the persisted openings, with one `finish` per receipt, plus the content opening at its first verification of an event, and records the verified commitment values with the event it holds; event replay compares those values and opens nothing. A key that carries an argument or result value has no committed-safe declaration; the lead's proposed third class for Chio-internal plaintext is not needed because no registered key carries a result after the projections below. Being generated by the kernel does not make a block committed-safe (Root-2 r2 request 2). `active_defense_body` and `response_simulation_report` are each the content preimage of their receipt and carry raw tenant and plan identifiers, so neither is registered. Each is replaced by a safe projection that P-d builds and tests and F switches on (option (a) of the request).

Field audit of `active_defense_body` (`ActiveDefenseReceiptBody`, 12 variants, at `fcc31d25fb`):

| Field | Variants | Class | Projection |
|---|---|---|---|
| `header.tenant_id: TenantId` | all 12 | raw tenant id | dropped from the projection; the receipt's signed `tenant_binding` is the one tenant representation (lead r4-delta e2 (g)) |
| `header.transition_id` | all 12 | domain hash over `{kind, tenant_id, action_id, plan_hash, expected_generation, mutation}`, no secret | keyed: `HMAC-SHA256(K[active-defense-digest], "chio.active-defense.keyed-digest.v1\0" || transition_id)` (purpose subkey, scheme in Task 5), carried as the `KeyedIndexValue { key_id, value }` that `TenantIndexKeySet::derive(IndexDomain::ActiveDefenseKeyedDigest, ..)` returns |
| `header.prior_receipt_ids` | all 12 | receipt ids | kept (receipt ids are public by spec) |
| `header.schema_version`, `occurred_at_unix_ms` | all 12 | metadata | kept |
| `policy.policy_version`, `policy.policy_hash` | policy-bound variants and `response.policy` | policy identity | kept |
| `response.action_id: ActionId` | ResponsePlan, ResponseStateTransition, EffectTransition, ResponseCompletion, LiftRollbackCompletion, SchedulerHealth | raw plan identifier | salted commitment, `salt_ref` `metadata.active_defense.action` |
| `response.trigger_finding_id`, `response.trigger_finding_receipt_id` | same six | raw finding identifiers | salted commitments, `metadata.active_defense.finding` and `metadata.active_defense.finding_receipt` |

| `response.affected_set_hash` | same six | `SHA-256(domain || canonical({tenant_id, affected_ids}))` with no secret: a dictionary oracle on tenant and subject ids | keyed as `transition_id` |
| `grant_id: GrantId` | DeclassificationConsumption, DeclassificationOutcome | raw grant identifier | salted commitment, `metadata.active_defense.grant` |
| `request_id: RequestId` | TripwireObservation | raw request identifier | salted commitment, `metadata.active_defense.request` |

Field audit of `response_simulation_report` (`ResponseSimulationReport`):

Field audit of `cage_receipt` (`CageReceiptBody`): `schema`, `attempt_id` (cage-minted), `stage` (enum), `bindings` (digests), `admitted_policy_digest`, `enforcement_record`, `started_at_unix_ms`, `recorded_at_unix_ms`. P-d audits `CageEnforcementRecord` member by member (`crates/security/chio-cage/tests/cage_receipt_disclosure_audit.rs`, #1727). The audit finds `cage_receipt` not committed-safe: `bindings.plan_digest` (and `enforcement_record.fully_enforced.prepared.plan_digest`) is an unsalted SHA-256 over the canonical cage-init plan, whose preimage holds the target argv, environment and descriptor paths, and `fd_table_digest` is an unsalted SHA-256 over the descriptor table with its paths. `cage_receipt` therefore stays unregistered in P-d, and the cage gets the A1 cage projection like `active_defense`, with the same tests; its openings persist with the cage receipt's append and the native launch evidence carries the two salt-only openings `cage_policy/evidence.rs` needs (Tasks 10 and 16). The receipt's top-level `tenant_id` from the cage signing context is dropped as for the two producers below.

Projection types, in `chio-core-types` beside `ActiveDefenseReceiptBody` and in `chio-kernel` beside `ResponseSimulationReport`:

```rust
/// Canonical JSON (RFC 8785) of one committed metadata field's value, zeroized on drop.
pub struct MetadataValue(Zeroizing<Vec<u8>>);
/// The salt and value of one `metadata.*` commitment (A1). Zeroized on drop; Debug prints neither.
pub struct MetadataOpening { pub salt: Salt, pub value: MetadataValue }
/// The openings of the `metadata.*` commitments one writer or projection built, keyed by `salt_ref`.
pub struct MetadataOpenings(BTreeMap<SaltRef, MetadataOpening>);
/// Signed metadata block `active_defense`. Same variant set as `ActiveDefenseReceiptBody`,
/// each identifier the audit marks raw replaced by a `MetadataCommitment` (A1 shape and domain,
/// `salt_ref` `metadata.active_defense.<field>`) and each keyed digest by a `KeyedIndexValue`.
pub enum ActiveDefenseReceiptProjection { /* 12 variants, closed structs, lossless_serde! */ }
impl ActiveDefenseReceiptBody {
    /// Draws one salt per committed identifier from `rng` and derives each keyed digest under the
    /// active generation of `keys` (`IndexDomain::ActiveDefenseKeyedDigest`); returns the
    /// projection and its openings.
    pub fn project_with(&self, keys: &TenantIndexKeySet, committer: &mut MetadataCommitter<'_>) -> Result<ActiveDefenseReceiptProjection>;
    /// True when every commitment in `projection` opens to this body through the receipt's
    /// verify-mode `committer` and every keyed digest derives under the generation its `key_id`
    /// names; false on any mismatch, missing opening or unknown `key_id`. The caller calls
    /// `committer.finish()` once per receipt, which refuses an unused opening.
    pub fn matches_with(&self, projection: &ActiveDefenseReceiptProjection, keys: &TenantIndexKeySet, committer: &mut MetadataCommitter<'_>) -> bool;
}
/// Signed metadata block `response_simulation`.
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct ResponseSimulationProjection { /* kept fields above and the three keyed digests (`KeyedIndexValue`) */ }
impl ResponseSimulationReport {
    pub fn project_with(&self, keys: &TenantIndexKeySet, committer: &mut MetadataCommitter<'_>) -> Result<ResponseSimulationProjection>;
    pub fn matches_with(&self, projection: &ResponseSimulationProjection, keys: &TenantIndexKeySet, committer: &mut MetadataCommitter<'_>) -> bool;
}
/// Signed metadata block `security_event`: `tenant_binding` from `keys.bind`, `subject` as a
/// `MetadataCommitment`, the Chio receipt and producer ids kept.
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct SecurityEventProjection { /* tenant_binding, subject commitment, source_receipt_id, producer_id */ }
impl SecurityEventProjection {
    pub fn project_with(body: &SecurityEventBody, keys: &TenantIndexKeySet, committer: &mut MetadataCommitter<'_>) -> Result<Self>;
    pub fn matches_with(&self, body: &SecurityEventBody, keys: &TenantIndexKeySet, committer: &mut MetadataCommitter<'_>) -> bool;
}
```

Projection commitment domain. Every projection commitment (`active_defense`, `response_simulation`, `security_event`) and the non-finding `delivery_contract.observed_digest` is a `MetadataCommitment` under the A1 domain `chio.receipt.metadata.v1\0 || utf8(salt_ref) || 0x00` with a `metadata.<block>.<field>` `salt_ref`; its preimage is the canonical JSON of the raw field value. P-d part 2 adds `METADATA_COMMITMENT_DOMAIN` and `CommitmentDomain::Metadata(&'a SaltRef)` beside #1782's variants, so a projection opening never verifies as a parameter, content, delegation-context or other metadata opening. The keyed digests take the deployment's `TenantIndexKeySet` explicitly: each producer and reader receives it at construction (`--tenant-index-keys`), never from ambient state, and a producer started without it refuses to start.

The projection openings reach the store with the receipt: the producer builds every block of a receipt through one project-mode `MetadataCommitter` and passes its `into_openings()` in `ReceiptDraft.metadata_openings`, the signer returns them in `SignedReceipt.openings.metadata` (Task 6), and `append_chio_receipt` writes each salt to `receipt_openings` and each value to `receipt_metadata_values` in the receipt's transaction (Task 4), under the `salt_ref`s named above. The producers run where a receipt store exists: the kernel's active-response coordinator and the control plane's security store. Both projections are registered `ChioInternal`, `CommittedSafe`. The full body and the full report stay the content preimages, so the content commitment hides them. Readers change in F (consumer kind F, Task 10): a reader that holds the full body or report (the coordinator, the control-plane security store) checks `matches_with` over a verify-mode `MetadataCommitter` on the persisted openings, with one `finish` per receipt, and opens the content commitment; a reader that holds only the receipt (lineage ingest, the security event consumer) uses the projection's digests and commitments and never sees a raw identifier. An erased or unknown projection opening fails a first verification closed with `OpeningUnavailable`; a holder's re-verification during recovery or re-projection compares the commitment values it recorded at first verification and opens nothing (Task 4 re-run rule).

Tenant binding (Root-2 r3). The top-level `tenant_id` that these producers set today is the signed tenant binding the store's authorization reads use: `receipt_query_snapshot/project.rs:101` derives the snapshot tenant from it, `receipt_query_snapshot/fetch.rs:87-94` refuses a snapshot tenant unlike it, and `receipt_store/reports/read_boundary/projection.rs:76` authenticates the stored tenant against it. F replaces the raw member for the native evidence, response simulation and cage producers with a signed opaque binding inside the signed body, so the store still authenticates tenant from the signature and no raw tenant id is published:

```rust
/// Signed body member. `value = HMAC-SHA256(K[tenant-binding], "chio.receipt.tenant-binding.v1\0" || tenant_id)`,
/// where `K[tenant-binding]` is the purpose subkey of the set key named by `key_id` (scheme below).
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct TenantBinding {
    pub alg: TenantBindingAlg,          // closed: "hkdf-hmac-sha256"
    pub key_id: Sha256Digest,           // SHA-256("chio.tenant-index-key-id.v1\0" || key)
    pub value: Sha256Digest,
}
/// Loaded from a 32-byte file at startup by every process that signs or reads tenant-scoped
/// receipts (the spec 7.12 tenant index key; item 4 reuses it for serve-http).
pub struct TenantIndexKey { /* zeroized on drop */ }
/// Closed set of purposes; each has its own HKDF subkey.
pub enum IndexPurpose { TenantBinding, TenantTag, SubjectTag, EnterpriseSubject, ActiveDefenseDigest }
/// Closed set of value domains (#1782); `IndexDomain::purpose` names the one purpose whose subkey
/// keys it, and `as_bytes` is its NUL-terminated domain constant.
pub enum IndexDomain { TenantBinding, TenantTag, SubjectTag, EnterpriseSubject, ScimCapability, ScimPolicy, ActiveDefenseKeyedDigest }
impl TenantIndexKeySet {
    /// Active generation only. Never used to check a stored binding; `verify` does that.
    pub fn bind(&self, tenant_id: &str) -> TenantBinding;
    /// Selects the generation by the binding's `key_id` (active or retired); constant-time
    /// comparison; refuses an unknown `key_id`.
    pub fn verify(&self, tenant_id: &str, binding: &TenantBinding) -> Result<(), TenantBindingError>;
    /// Keyed value of `domain || preimage` under the active generation, keyed by the subkey of the
    /// domain's purpose, and its verifier across generations.
    pub fn derive(&self, domain: IndexDomain, preimage: &[u8]) -> KeyedIndexValue;
    pub fn verify_value(&self, domain: IndexDomain, preimage: &[u8], value: &KeyedIndexValue) -> Result<(), TenantBindingError>;
    /// Keyed value under the generation named by `key_id`; refuses an unknown `key_id` with
    /// `TenantBindingError::TenantIndexKeyUnknown`. Used by the all-generation match mode.
    pub fn derive_in(&self, key_id: &Sha256Digest, domain: IndexDomain, preimage: &[u8]) -> Result<KeyedIndexValue, TenantBindingError>;
    /// The loaded generations, active first, then retired.
    pub fn key_ids(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &Sha256Digest>;
}
pub struct KeyedIndexValue { pub key_id: Sha256Digest, pub value: Sha256Digest }
```

The binding is keyed, so a holder of the receipt without the key cannot test a tenant guess, and it is deterministic, so the store verifies it without any opening; erasure of openings never affects it, and nothing falls back to trusting the column. `ChioReceiptBody` gains `tenant_binding: Option<TenantBinding>`; a body that carries both `tenant_id` and `tenant_binding`, or `authority` with either, fails signing. Other producers keep `tenant_id` as spec 7.6 allows; moving them to the binding is open question 11. Checks:

| Reader | Check after F |
|---|---|
| append (`SqliteReceiptStore`, trust-control, replication import) | the tenant column is written from `SignedReceipt.tenant` and checked by the tenant rule below (`TenantIndexKeySet::verify` for a binding, equality for a signed `tenant_id`) before the row commits; any mismatch refuses |
| snapshot project (`project.rs:101`) | the snapshot tenant comes from the stored column and is verified against the signed binding; a mismatch refuses the projection |
| snapshot fetch (`fetch.rs:87-94`) | a snapshot tenant is accepted only when `verify` succeeds against the receipt's signed binding |
| read boundary (`read_boundary/projection.rs:76`) | the stored tenant projection is authenticated with `verify` |
| evidence export and the internal opening read | tenant-scoped selection uses the verified column; the internal read derives the tenant from the caller credential and checks the receipt's binding with `TenantIndexKeySet::verify` (Task 4) |
| every other receipt tenant reader | per the census TN kinds: TN-C reads the verified column, TN-B verifies the binding, TN-U is unchanged |

Tenant source (lead r4-delta e2 (c), (d)). The receipt no longer carries the tenant in plaintext, so the tenant travels beside it as unsigned context that never enters the signed body: `SignedReceipt` gains `tenant: Option<TenantId>`, set by the producer from its own context. It travels on the remote append request, in cluster openings and receipt deltas and in snapshots (every node verifies, so every node holds the key set), and in the admission row before publication. Append and every replication import enforce one rule before the row commits: a signed `tenant_binding` requires a present `tenant` that verifies; a signed `tenant_id` requires `tenant` equal to it; neither signed form requires `tenant` absent and the column NULL; anything else refuses. No receipt query treats a NULL tenant as visible to a tenant-scoped reader (test `null_tenant_not_visible_to_tenant_scoped_reads`).

Key set and provisioning (lead r4-delta e2 (e)). `TenantIndexKeySet { active: TenantIndexKey, retired: Vec<TenantIndexKey> }`: `bind` uses the active key; `verify` selects the key by the binding's `key_id` across the active and retired generations and refuses an unknown `key_id`. One key set per deployment, generated by the new `chio keys tenant-index generate --out <dir>` (F), provisioned by the operator as a directory (`active`, `retired/<key_id>`) to every process of the deployment that signs or reads tenant-scoped receipts: trust-control, the kernel-hosting processes (`--tenant-index-keys <dir>`), the control plane's security services and every cluster node (operators provision each node; the cluster does not replicate keys). Startup refuses a missing active key, a key length other than 32 bytes, a duplicate `key_id`, or a file readable by group or other or not owned by the process user (spec 8.3). Rotation generates a new active key and moves the old one to `retired`; retired keys only verify. Removing a retired key makes its bindings unverifiable, so tenant-scoped reads of those receipts refuse; the operations guide says to retain retired keys for the receipt retention period. Keys never leave a process and are never signing keys. Tests: `tenant_index_keys_refuse_wrong_length`, `..._refuse_loose_permissions`, `..._refuse_duplicate_key_id`, `tenant_binding_verifies_after_rotation`, `tenant_binding_unknown_key_id_refuses`.

Purpose subkeys (lead decision, #1728 and #1731). One rotating key set, the `--tenant-index-keys <dir>` set above, serves every keyed index value in M1. Each purpose uses its own subkey, never the set key directly:

- **Derivation.** `K[purpose] = HKDF-SHA256(IKM = set key, salt = "chio.tenant-index.v1", info = "chio.tenant-index.v1/" || label, L = 32)` (RFC 5869, the `hkdf` crate already in `Cargo.lock`), computed once per generation at load and zeroized on drop. A value is `HMAC-SHA256(K[purpose], purpose-domain || 0x00 || preimage)`. HKDF over an HMAC-with-prefix scheme: each purpose gets an independent pseudorandom key, so separation does not depend on every purpose choosing non-overlapping preimage encodings, and an HMAC result obtained under one purpose (an oracle, a log, a stored value) is never an input that verifies under another. The extract step also tolerates a future set key that is not uniformly random.
- **Labels (exact, closed).** `tenant-binding` (item 2, `TenantBinding`), `tenant-tag` (item 4, the anchored-root and receipt tenant tag, #1731), `subject-tag` (item 4, the anchored-root subject tag matched by item 5 revocation entries, #1731), `enterprise-subject` (P-g, enterprise subject identity and the SCIM capability id and policy hash), `active-defense-digest` (item 2 Task 5, the active-defense keyed digests and keyed evidence ids). Adding a purpose adds a label in this list and in PROTOCOL 6; no purpose reuses another's label.
- **Key id.** `key_id = SHA-256("chio.tenant-index-key-id.v1\0" || set key)` names the set generation, not the subkey. Every derived value carries it: typed values as `KeyedIndexValue { key_id, value }` (`TenantBinding` adds `alg`); a value carried as a string (an enterprise subject id, a SCIM capability id suffix) uses `<prefix>1.<hex key_id>.<hex value>`, with prefix `tb`, `tt`, `st`, `esk`, `ad` per purpose, and is parsed exactly (lowercase hex, two dots, fixed lengths), refusing anything else.
- **Rotation and verification.** `derive` uses the active generation. `verify_value` and `verify` select the generation by `key_id` across active and retired, derive that generation's purpose subkey and compare in constant time. An unknown `key_id` refuses (`TenantIndexKeyUnknown`); there is no fallback to the active key or to an unkeyed form. Equality readers that compare two stored values (cross-node matching of enterprise subjects, tenant tag joins) compare `(key_id, value)` pairs; values from different generations are never compared by value: a holder of the preimage re-derives under the active key, and enterprise identities resolve through P-g's lookup contract (identity id, re-key on rotation, history).
- **Rotation modes.** A purpose rotates in one of two ways, fixed per purpose. Re-derive with history (`enterprise-subject`, P-g): the store holds the preimage, so on rotation it re-derives every stored value under the new active generation in one transaction and keeps the prior `(key_id, value)` pairs in a history table, so older receipts still join. All-generation match (`tenant-tag`, `subject-tag`, item 4): the store keeps no plaintext, so a lookup or revocation match computes the candidate from the request or admin plaintext under every loaded generation (`key_ids()` with `derive_in`) and compares each against the stored value with its `key_id`; every retired generation that a stored value references stays loaded, startup refuses when one is missing, and an unknown `key_id` refuses. `tenant-binding` and `active-defense-digest` are verified against a known preimage, so they select the generation by `key_id` and need neither mode.
- **Purpose isolation.** The verifier for a field derives the subkey for that field's own purpose only; the purpose is fixed by the field's type, not read from the value. Test `index_value_never_verifies_under_another_purpose`: a value derived for each label, over the same preimage and the same generation, refuses under every other label. Tests `index_value_verifies_across_generations`, `index_value_unknown_key_id_refuses`, `index_value_string_form_parses_exactly`.

Linkability (lead r4-delta e2 (f)). The binding is deterministic per tenant and key, so any holder of several receipts can group them by tenant without learning the tenant. This is accepted and stated in PROTOCOL 6 next to the "not a dictionary oracle" property.

`TenantBinding` and `TenantIndexKeySet` are additive types and land in P-b; F adds the body member, `SignedReceipt.tenant` and the checks.

A required block that would carry argument or result values is redesigned in F to a committed-safe typed form or the producer fails closed (Root-2 condition 5); Task 1 lists any such block, and an unexpected one stops the lane. The scattered key literals move to the registry constants; PROTOCOL 6.4 gains the normative key table; the kernel's reserved-key collision assertion and metadata-is-object assertion run before body construction. Dropping and refusal stay off in P-d and are switched on in F (Task 8).

- [ ] **Step 1: Failing tests:** `unsafe_required_guard_refuses_registration`; `security_projections_carry_no_raw_identifiers` (random marker tenant, action, finding, grant, request, submitter, subject and affected ids through every `ActiveDefenseReceiptBody` variant, a `ResponseSimulationReport` and a `SecurityEventBody`; no marker raw, hex or base64 in any projection); `kept_digests_are_keyed_or_entropy_backed` (for each kept digest, derive the unkeyed digest from the marker inputs and assert it does not appear in the projection; a digest kept unkeyed must have a named high-entropy input that the test randomizes); `projection_opens_to_its_body`; `projection_keyed_digests_verify_after_rotation` (project under one generation and rotate: `matches_with` plus `finish` succeeds with the retired generation loaded and fails with it removed); `projection_commitment_refuses_cross_domain_opening` (a parameters, content, delegation-context or other `metadata.*` opening over the same salt and value does not open a projection commitment); `every_guard_declares` (compiles only when every impl declares); `wasm_guard_declares_unsafe`; `registry_covers_census_keys` (every key the census found is registered); `reserved_key_from_caller_refuses`.
