### Task 5b: P-e, every committed-receipt raw decode through `UntrustedJsonText::decode_signed` (shared fix, lead-owned in the fwdcompat lane)

`serde_json::Value` keeps the last of two duplicate keys, so a duplicate key inside a `Value`-typed member (`metadata`, `parameters_inline`, `result_inline`, an opening's `payload`) collapses when a committed-receipt type is decoded straight from raw bytes with `serde_json::from_str`, `from_slice` or a raw `T::deserialize`. Main already has the strict entry: `chio-core-types/src/canonical/untrusted.rs` `UntrustedJsonText::decode_signed` parses through `signed_json::StoredValue`, which refuses duplicate keys at every depth before they reach a map, keeps full-width integers and integer-valued floats (the native signed numeric domain), and has 327 production call sites. `decode_document` is the duplicate-refusing entry for unsigned documents. P-e adds no decoder.

**Contract (one strict entry plus a gate).**
- Every production raw decode of a committed-receipt type (receipts, receipt bodies, disclosure blocks, projections, the relay inputs, trust-control route bodies, receipt-store row decodes, SDK verifier entry points that sit in Rust) goes through `UntrustedJsonText::decode_signed`. Opening documents (`chio.receipt-disclosure.v1`) go through `decode_document`.
- The public `Deserialize` impls stay, and the plan does not claim that a direct `T::deserialize` over raw bytes refuses a duplicate nested inside a `Value` member. The gate keeps such calls off production paths.
- Strict types are transitive (lead r5 N2): a type is strict when it is in `COMMITTED_RECEIPT_STRICT_TYPES` or contains one at any depth (`Vec<ChioReceipt>`, `Option<..>`, maps, tuples, `SignedExportEnvelope<..>`, request and response structs with a receipt member). The list is generated by a module walk in `chio-core-types` tests and exported for the gate.
- Gate: `scripts/check-committed-receipt-raw-decode.py` finds every non-test decode whose target is a strict type outside `UntrustedJsonText::decode_signed` and `decode_document`: `serde_json::from_str`, `from_slice`, `from_reader`, `serde_json::Deserializer::from_*` feeding a `Deserialize` call, a decode to `serde_json::Value` followed by `from_value`, and serde-driven extractors (`axum::Json<T>`, `axum::extract::Query<T>`, `axum::Form<T>`, and any other extractor implemented with serde). Such routes use a new `SignedJson<T>` extractor whose body decode calls `decode_signed` (the remote append route `receipt_handlers.rs:121` first among them). The allowlist is the two entry points and `SignedJson`.

**Tests.**
- `raw_duplicate_keys_refused_by_decode_signed`: for every type in `COMMITTED_RECEIPT_STRICT_TYPES`, bytes with a duplicate key at the top level, in each nested Chio object and inside each `Value`-typed member refuse through `decode_signed` (and each public concrete decoder that wraps it) with the exact duplicate-key error.
- `decode_signed_keeps_native_numeric_domain`: a receipt whose arguments carry a u64 above 2^53 and a `100.0` float decodes through `decode_signed` and re-encodes byte-identically, so P-e refuses nothing the native signed domain accepts.
- `opening_document_duplicates_refused_by_decode_document`.
- `http_append_route_refuses_duplicate_keys`: a `POST /v1/receipts/tools` body with a duplicate key inside a receipt's `metadata` is refused by `SignedJson` before the handler runs.
- `strict_type_list_is_transitive`: a container of a strict type appears in the exported list.
- `direct_raw_decode_of_receipt_types_is_gated`: the gate's behavior on a fixture tree with a raw `serde_json::from_str::<ChioReceipt>` outside the entry points (fails) and inside them (passes), run as part of the gate script's own invocation, not a separate test file.
- The SDK verifiers (Task 17) run the same raw duplicate cases.

- [ ] **Run** `cs cargo test -p chio-core-types --test receipt_strict_decode`, the changed decoders' crates, `python3 scripts/check-committed-receipt-raw-decode.py`, and `cs cargo xtask gen signed-fixtures --check` (no change), then the Verification matrix for every PR.

### Task 5c: P-f, trust-control caller credentials and an internal listener (precursor)

Trust-control today has one bearer secret, `config.service_token`, checked by `validate_service_auth` (`report_validation.rs:263-281`) on every client route and keying the cluster peer HMAC, plus per-tenant `tenant_read_tokens` (`service_types/config.rs:73`), and serves one router on one listener (`service_runtime/init.rs:325`), including the existing `/v1/internal/*` routes (`router.rs:373,389`). The internal opening read and salt-bearing replication need scoped, attributable credentials and a listener the public cannot reach (lead r4-delta e3). P-f builds them before P-c.

**Interfaces.**
- Config: `[[credentials]]` entries `{ id, kind: "caller" | "deployment", secret_sha256, scopes: [..], deployment, tenant_authority: "deployment" | { tenant = ".." } }`, where `kind` is required (no default; a missing or unknown kind refuses at load), and `[cluster.peers]` entries `{ node_id, internal_url, public_key, scopes: [..] }`, where `scopes` is required, non-empty and drawn only from `cluster:replicate` and `cluster-openings:replicate` (any other scope refuses at load). A peer reaches a `cluster-openings:replicate` route only when its entry lists that scope. `service_token` and `tenant_read_tokens` are removed. The operator rewrites the configuration: the release notes and `chio trust config check` show the credential entries that reproduce today's access (the old service token's routes as one credential per caller role, each tenant read token as a `Tenant(t)` credential with `receipts:read`, `reports:read` and `evidence:read`, so a migrated tenant keeps evidence export). Nothing is migrated automatically, and a configuration that still names `service_token` or `tenant_read_tokens` refuses at startup.
- Route authorization table (Root-2 r5). Today's `json_ingress::authenticate` middleware (`json_ingress.rs:276-312`) maps JSON ingress paths to an authentication class, and handlers recheck. P-f turns that map into one table covering every route on both listeners, enforced by the `authorize_route` middleware (mounted with `route_layer` on both routers, so it runs for every request that matches a mounted route, not only JSON ingress) and rechecked by `validate_caller(required_scope)` in each handler. A request for a mounted route absent from the table, or a credential naming a scope absent from the closed scope set, refuses. A request that matches no mounted route reaches no handler and no trust-control state: the internal router has no fallback and answers 404, and the public router answers 404 unless the dashboard is built, in which case its one fallback serves the dashboard's static files (the fallback row after the table). The table below is generated from the router at `origin/main` `2bfbbfff2b` by `docs/superpowers/plans/m1-item2-routes/trust_control_routes.py` (committed with its rules `scope_rules.tsv` and output `routes-2bfbbfff2b.tsv`; 217 routes: 210 mounted, all in the table, 0 unruled, and 7 unmounted listed apart in `routes-2bfbbfff2b-unmounted.tsv`). It fails closed (Root-2 r6): it verifies the revision is a commit, exits 2 on any git failure other than an empty grep, and exits 1 when it finds no routes, when a route has no rule, when a matched rule yields `UNMAPPED`, or when a path constant or handler does not resolve. Path constants resolve by crate: a name qualified or imported from a crate resolves in that crate, an unqualified name resolves to the trust-control crate's own definition first, and a name defined only in several other crates is unresolved (exit 1). `trust_control_routes.py --self-check <repo> <rev> <workdir>` requires a nonexistent revision, a rule set whose only rule is the `UNMAPPED` fallback, and an empty rule set each to exit nonzero, and checks the resolver on a same-named constant from another crate (`chio-http-core`'s `/emergency-stop` against trust-control's `/v1/admin/emergency-stop`), a qualified name, a name defined in two other crates and an unknown name. It parses every `.route(` call in the trust-control router sources, resolves path constants and aliases, finds the router function that contains each call and treats a route as mounted only when non-test code outside that function's definition names it, and records the authentication helper each handler (or a function it calls, two levels deep) uses today, so each row's credential kind follows today's check. Rows marked "unchanged" keep their existing non-credential authentication (anonymous public metadata, finding seller signatures, OID4VCI wallet tokens). `frost_coordinator_router` (`frost/coordinator.rs:565`) has no caller, so its seven `/v1/frost/coordinator/sessions/*` routes are unmounted: they are not in the table, and since the middleware refuses any route absent from the table, mounting that router later fails closed until its rows are added with their scopes. The four rows marked "new in this plan" are the routes Task 4 adds. The row marked "added by item 4 D2" is the trust-control exchange signer route that item 4 PR D2 adds; it lands with D2, not with P-f. Until D2 merges, `scope_rules.tsv` has no rule for it and the generator does not list it in `routes-<rev>.tsv`, so the 217-route count above is unchanged and the route is not mounted. `root-exchange:sign` is in the closed scope set from P-f, so a credential naming it loads, but no route accepts it before D2. D2 adds the `scope_rules.tsv` rule and regenerates `routes-<rev>.tsv` in the same PR, and the row in this table then matches the generated output.

Fallback, listed apart because it is a `fallback_service` mount, not a `.route(` call:

| Method | Route | Listener | Credential | Scope | Tenant authority |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| any | any path no mounted route matches, mounted only when `dashboard/dist/index.html` exists at startup | public | anonymous | `none` | n/a (`router.rs` `fallback_service(ServeDir::new(DASHBOARD_DIST_DIR).not_found_service(ServeFile::new(index.html)))`: static dashboard files, no handler, no state; unchanged) |

The dashboard's API calls reach table routes and present their own credentials. P-f extends `trust_control_routes.py` and `route_table_covers_every_router_route` to scan the router sources for `fallback_service(`, `.fallback(`, `nest_service(` and `.nest(` mounts as well, and to fail on any mount other than this one fallback on the public router.

Closed scope set: `admin:emergency`, `admission-authority:write`, `attestation:write`, `authority:admin`, `authority:read`, `budgets:read`, `budgets:write`, `capabilities:issue`, `certifications:read`, `certifications:write`, `cluster:replicate`, `cluster-openings:replicate`, `credit:read`, `credit:write`, `evidence:read`, `evidence:write`, `federation:read`, `federation:write`, `findings:read`, `findings:write`, `fiscal:read`, `fiscal:write`, `lineage:read`, `lineage:write`, `metrics:read`, `passport:read`, `passport:write`, `receipt-openings:erase`, `receipt-openings:read`, `receipts:admin`, `receipts:append`, `receipts:read`, `registry:write`, `reports:read`, `reputation:read`, `reputation:write`, `revocations:read`, `revocations:write`, `root-exchange:sign`, `scim:write`, `settlement:write`. Credential kinds: `caller` (an operator or service client), `deployment` (a kernel-hosting process or control-plane service of one deployment), `peer` (a cluster node, pinned Ed25519 key, internal listener only); a `peer` credential is refused on every public route and a `caller` or `deployment` credential on every peer route.
- `validate_caller(&headers, required_scope) -> Result<AuthenticatedCaller>` with `AuthenticatedCaller { credential_id, deployment, tenant_authority, scopes }`, constant-time secret comparison, used by every route.
- Internal listener: a second bind address `internal_listen`. Every `/v1/internal/*` route (the existing receipt delta and cluster snapshot routes, the new openings stream and openings read) is mounted only on the internal router; the public router has none of them. Peers use `internal_url`.
- Peer auth: `validate_cluster_peer_auth` verifies an Ed25519 signature by the claimed node's pinned key over the payload above (sender and intended receiver node ids, deployment id, method, path, raw query, issue time, term, body digest and a single-use nonce), and refuses a receiver or deployment other than its own.

**Tests:** `route_table_covers_every_router_route` (the router's route list equals the table's), `unknown_route_refuses`, `credential_with_unknown_scope_refuses_at_startup`, `read_scope_cannot_write` (for each family, a `:read` credential on its write routes refuses), `write_scope_cannot_read_other_family`, `tenant_credential_cross_tenant_read_refused` (a `Tenant(a)` credential on `/v1/receipts/query`, `/v1/receipts/tools` and the reports for tenant b), `tenant_credential_refused_on_deployment_only_routes`, `peer_credential_refused_on_public_routes`, `caller_credential_refused_on_peer_routes`, `deployment_credential_of_other_deployment_refused`, `public_router_has_no_internal_paths`, `internal_listener_refuses_without_credential`, `scope_required_per_route`, `existing_client_routes_keep_access_under_mapped_scopes`, `tenant_credential_limited_to_its_tenant`, `service_token_secret_no_longer_authenticates_cluster_routes`, `peer_signature_from_unpinned_key_refused`, `old_config_keys_refuse_at_startup`, `dashboard_fallback_serves_only_static_files` (with a built dashboard directory, an unmatched `/v1/` path returns the index file and reaches no handler), `internal_router_has_no_fallback`, `route_scan_refuses_an_unlisted_fallback_mount`.

- [ ] **Run** `cs cargo test -p chio-control-plane --no-fail-fast`, `cs cargo test -p chio-cli --no-fail-fast` (P-f changes the trust-control configuration surface of the CLI), clippy for both, `python3 -I docs/superpowers/plans/m1-item2-routes/trust_control_routes.py --self-check <repo> <rev> <workdir>`, the generator at the PR base with no unruled route or mount, and the Verification matrix for every PR.

**Size:** 3-5 days, counted in the Size table. It changes trust-control configuration for every deployment (D17 item 8).

### Task 6: F commits 1 and 2, committed body and the portable signer

**Files:** `crates/core/chio-core-types/src/receipt/{body,decision,kinds,metadata,signing}.rs`, `tests/committed_receipt_negatives.rs` (new); `crates/kernel/chio-kernel-core/src/{receipts,lib,kani_public_harnesses}.rs`; contract move-in files.

**Interfaces:**

```rust
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct ToolCallAction {
    pub parameter_commitment: ReceiptCommitment,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub parameters_inline: Option<serde_json::Map<String, serde_json::Value>>,
}
// ToolCallAction::from_parameters and verify_hash are deleted; canonical_parameter_digest
// (consumer kind A0) is the request digest helper.
pub fn canonical_parameter_digest(arguments: &serde_json::Value) -> Result<Sha256Hex>;

// ChioReceiptBody: removed redaction_mode, content_hash; added
//   content_commitment: ReceiptCommitment, result_inline: Option<Map>,
//   disclosure: DisclosureBlock, authority: Option<ReceiptAuthority>
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct DisclosureBlock { pub policy_digest: Option<Sha256Digest>,
    pub parameter_inline_fields: Vec<JsonPointer>, pub result_inline_fields: Vec<JsonPointer> }
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct ReceiptAuthority { pub root_anchor: RootAnchor, pub chain_digest: Sha256Digest,
    pub chain_depth: u32, pub holder: HolderClass }

pub struct ReceiptDraft { /* id, timestamp, capability_id, tool_server, tool_name, decision,
    receipt_kind, boundary_class, observation_outcome, tool_origin, actor_chain, policy_hash,
    evidence, metadata, metadata_openings: MetadataOpenings, trust_level, tenant_id, tenant_binding,
    tenant: Option<TenantId> (unsigned, never in the body), authority, kernel_key, bbs_projection_version */ }
pub struct ReceiptPreimages<'a> { pub parameters: &'a serde_json::Value, pub content: ContentPreimage<'a> }
/// Every opening of one signed receipt, all of which its append writes: the parameter and content
/// salts and each `metadata.*` commitment's salt and canonical value, projection fields included
/// (Task 5, A1). Zeroized on drop; Debug prints the receipt id and the `salt_ref`s only.
pub struct ReceiptOpenings { pub receipt_id: String, pub parameters: Salt, pub content: Salt, pub metadata: MetadataOpenings }
impl ReceiptOpenings {
    /// `params` and `content` only; metadata salts and values never leave the store through it (A1).
    pub fn salt_only(&self) -> ReceiptDisclosure;
    /// Value outputs only (Task 3 payload forms); `params` and `content` only.
    pub fn with_value_payloads(&self, parameters: &serde_json::Value, content: &serde_json::Value) -> ReceiptDisclosure;
}
/// `tenant` is unsigned context the producer sets from its own context (Task 5 tenant source).
/// It never enters the signed body and travels with the receipt to every append, the remote
/// append request, the admission row, receipt deltas and snapshots.
#[must_use]
pub struct SignedReceipt { pub receipt: ChioReceipt, pub openings: ReceiptOpenings, pub tenant: Option<TenantId> }

impl ChioReceipt {
    pub fn sign(draft: ReceiptDraft, preimages: ReceiptPreimages<'_>, rng: &dyn Rng, keypair: &Keypair) -> Result<SignedReceipt>;
    pub fn sign_with_backend(draft: ReceiptDraft, preimages: ReceiptPreimages<'_>, rng: &dyn Rng, backend: &dyn SigningBackend) -> Result<SignedReceipt>;
    pub fn sign_with_backend_using_handle(draft: ReceiptDraft, parameters: &serde_json::Value, rng: &dyn Rng, backend: &dyn SigningBackend, handle: ReceiptSigningHandle) -> Result<SignedReceipt>;
    pub fn sign_prepared_with_bbs_using_handle(draft: ReceiptDraft, parameters: &serde_json::Value, rng: &dyn Rng, backend: &dyn SigningBackend, handle: ReceiptSigningHandle, bbs: &BbsSigner) -> Result<SignedReceipt>;
    pub fn open(&self, disclosure: &ReceiptDisclosure, own_parameters: Option<&serde_json::Value>, own_output: Option<ContentPreimage<'_>>) -> OpeningReport;
}

// chio-kernel-core
pub enum ReceiptSigningError { ContentCommitmentMismatch { salt_ref: SaltRef }, KernelKeyMismatch,
    SaltGeneration(RngError), DuplicateSalt, TenantIdWithAuthority, InlineFieldsWithoutPolicy,
    UnregisteredMetadataKey { key: String }, UnopenedCommitment { salt_ref: SaltRef },
    OrphanOpening { salt_ref: SaltRef }, MetadataCommitmentMismatch { salt_ref: SaltRef }, SigningFailed(String) }
pub fn sign_receipt(draft: ReceiptDraft, preimages: ReceiptPreimages<'_>, rng: &dyn Rng, backend: &dyn SigningBackend) -> Result<SignedReceipt, ReceiptSigningError>;
pub fn sign_receipt_with_handle(draft: ReceiptDraft, parameters: &serde_json::Value, rng: &dyn Rng, backend: &dyn SigningBackend, handle: ReceiptSigningHandle) -> Result<SignedReceipt, ReceiptSigningError>;
```

Signer order: kernel key check against `backend.public_key()?`; metadata registry check over `draft.metadata` (`check_internal_receipt_metadata` from P-d: every top-level key registered, `ChioInternal` and `CommittedSafe`, else `UnregisteredMetadataKey { key }`, with no RNG or backend call); two salt draws; metadata openings merge from `ReceiptDraft.metadata_openings` (each `MetadataCommitment` in the draft has exactly one opening, else `UnopenedCommitment`; each opening has a commitment, else `OrphanOpening`; each opening recomputes its commitment, else `MetadataCommitmentMismatch`); salt equality across the parameter, content and every metadata salt (`DuplicateSalt`); commitment computation; recompute check (`ContentCommitmentMismatch`); body validation (`validate_signable_semantics`, `tenant_id` rule, inline-field rule); wrapped signing. `ChioReceipt::sign*` in core-types runs the same order and returns `Error::UnregisteredMetadataKey(key)` and the matching core errors; no receipt signer skips the registry check. Deleted: `sign_receipt_relaying_trusted_body`, `ContentHashMismatch`, every signer that takes a `ChioReceiptBody`, `RedactionMode`, `ReceiptSemanticFields.redaction_mode`. `ReceiptSigningHandle` owns a `ContentPreimage`. Every producer sets `authority: None` (open question 3).

Contract move-in (in-place schemas README): `git mv` the staged `record.schema.json` over the live file after removing `enforcement`, the `pdp_decision`, `prevent_external` and `delegated` values and rule, and `settlement`; re-diff against live and carry in #1715's closed layers and item 1's `algorithm` removal first. `content_commitment` is required. Register `urn:chio:error:attest:receipt-tenant-id-with-authority`. Regenerate code in four languages.

- [ ] **Step 1: Failing tests** in `committed_receipt_negatives.rs` and `receipts.rs`: `equal_salts_refuse`, `rng_failure_refuses_signing` (no backend call), `tenant_id_with_authority_refuses_signing`, `padded_blocks_fail_decoding` (each new block and `authority.root_anchor`), `removed_members_fail_decoding` (`content_hash`, `redaction_mode`, `action.parameters`, `action.parameter_hash`), `commitment_salt_ref_checked_by_position`, `kernel_key_mismatch_refuses_before_salts`, `receipt_signers_take_preimages` (calls every public receipt signer in core-types, kernel-core and selective-disclosure with the new signatures).
- [ ] **Strictness gate (mandatory).** `crates/core/chio-core-types/tests/receipt_strict_decode.rs` (from #1715) gains `committed_receipt_types_refuse_padding_and_lossy_decode`, driven by `COMMITTED_RECEIPT_STRICT_TYPES`, which lists every committed-receipt type: `ChioReceipt`, `ChioReceiptBody`, `ChioReceiptIdInput`, `ChioReceiptSigningBody`, `ToolCallAction`, `ReceiptCommitment`, `CommitmentAlg`, `DisclosureBlock`, `ReceiptAuthority` (with `RootAnchor` and `HolderClass`), `Decision`, `OperationTerminalState`, `GuardEvidence`, `ActorRef`, `ReceiptDisclosure`, `Opening`, `TenantBinding`, `ActiveDefenseReceiptProjection` (each variant), `ResponseSimulationProjection`, the relay `content` input union and the HTTP `HttpBody` tag. For each type, in the shape of #1723's concrete-path tests: a padded member at every nesting level, a duplicate member at a Chio-object level (refused by the derived impls on raw paths; duplicates nested inside `Value`-typed members are refused by `decode_signed` and covered by P-e's tests, not by direct `T::deserialize`; `from_value` cannot express a duplicate key), and for each tagged variant (unit variants included) a padded variant such as `{"verdict":"allow","pad":0}`, each refused with the exact error, through `T::deserialize(&mut serde_json::Deserializer::from_str(..))`, `serde_json::from_str::<T>`, `serde_json::from_value::<T>` and every public concrete decoder of the type; and a lossless round trip (the decoded value re-encodes to exactly the input). A type missing from the list fails `committed_receipt_strict_types_cover_the_module` (a module walk over the public receipt and disclosure types). The same cases run against #1716's SDK decoders in Task 17.

### Task 7: F commits 3, 4 and 6, hosted kernel salts and internal consumers

**Files:** `crates/kernel/chio-kernel/src/{kernel/construction.rs,kernel_struct.rs,receipt_support/**,kernel/responses/**,kernel/signing_task.rs,kernel/admission_terminal_receipt.rs,kernel/effect_obligation/**,kernel/kernel_drop_guard.rs,kernel/reconciliation.rs,kernel/session_ops/**,kernel/admission_coordinator/**,admission_operation/**,tool_outcome/caller_delivery.rs,kernel/verified_treaty.rs,kernel/nonce_admission.rs,kernel/validation/tool_output.rs,kernel/settlement_observer.rs}`, `chio-store-sqlite` admission operation rows, `chio-secret-broker` kernel host, `chio-control-plane/src/lib.rs` (`build_kernel_with_clock`, `configure_receipt_store`), `chio-api-protect/src/proxy/mediated.rs`.

- Kernel construction carries `rng: Arc<dyn Rng + Send + Sync>` in `KernelConfig` (one constructor input, no setter), defaulting nowhere: every one of the 421 construction sites passes it (release code `OsEntropyRng`, tests `OsEntropyRng` or `FixtureRng`). A durable receipt store, local or remote, always stores openings (Task 4), so no "store without disclosure store" state exists (P1-1).
- Commit 3 wires the kernel `Rng` into all 20 kernel producer rows (one producer). `api protect`'s mediated kernel and the secret broker's hosted kernel are this producer; commit 6 wires the broker host's `Rng` and store.
- Commit 4 applies consumer kinds A, A0 and B to every kernel and store row in the Task 1 table. The admission operation row gains `receipt_parameter_commitment` and `receipt_content_commitment`, written in the same transaction that records the terminal receipt, and `request_namespace_salt`, `receipt_namespace_commitment`, `request_binding_salt` and `receipt_request_binding_commitment`, written at admission (A1 "Request namespace commitment" and the `admission_operation` paragraph), and the re-run columns listed below; the row's existing `action_parameter_hash` becomes the `canonical_parameter_digest` value; the retained output blob keeps its own digest. Compacted replay compares `sha256(blob)` with the row's output digest and the receipt's commitment values with the row's commitment values; it never requires an opening, and when `opening_status` is `Present` it also reads the opening and recomputes the content commitment over the blob (kind A).
- Kernel paths that need an opening are kind E-k (`allow_responses.rs`) and the optional compacted-replay recompute. E-k fails with `KernelError::ReceiptOpeningUnavailable(OpeningUnavailable)` when the opening is erased, unknown or the remote read is refused; compacted replay skips the recompute in those states and never fails on them. E-k opens only at the memory-write operation's first execution.
- Re-run sites (Task 4 rule). Each check opens only at first execution and records what its re-runs compare; every new column is part of its store's fresh-only v1 catalog:
  - Admission replay, refusal replay, completed-receipt validation and compacted replay compare the receipt with the admission row's `receipt_parameter_commitment`, `receipt_content_commitment`, `receipt_namespace_commitment`, `receipt_request_binding_commitment` and, for a committed request id, `receipt_request_id_commitment` (kind A, A1).
  - Participant evidence (`validate_economic_receipt_obligation`, A1 `governed_transaction` rows): the first run opens `seller`, `settlement_destination` and `payee_binding` and verifies them against the atom; in the transaction that records the participant projection the admission row records `receipt_seller_commitment`, `receipt_settlement_destination_commitment` and `receipt_payee_binding_commitment`, and the projection keeps the atom and its `source_record_digest`. Re-validation during recovery or replay compares the receipt's values with these columns and the atom with its recorded digest.
  - Finding memory write (kind E-k, `allow_responses.rs:547,879`): the first execution opens the parent's content commitment over the written content, and the memory-write operation's admission row records `parent_content_commitment` (the parent's value it verified) beside the `written_content_digest` it holds. Recovery compares the parent receipt's value with that column and the content with the row's digest.
  - Completed result (kind A/E, `completed_result.rs:133,154`): the first verification opens, and the session credential call row records `receipt_params_commitment`, `receipt_content_commitment`, for a committed request id `receipt_request_id_commitment`, and the delivered output's `output_sha256` beside the call arguments it holds. A replayed completed result compares the receipt's values with these columns and the output with `output_sha256`.
  - Finding pool ledger (A1 `finding_pool_mutation`): at mutation time the ledger verifies the receipt in process with `SignedReceipt.openings` and records `receipt_parameter_commitment` and `receipt_content_commitment` in the mutation's `finding_pool_receipt_outbox` row; the mutation's plaintext stays in its own `finding_pool_debits` and `finding_pool_allocations` rows. Ledger replay rebuilds the expected mutation from those rows and compares the outbox receipt's values with the outbox columns.
  - `delivery_contract.observed_digest` (Task 5): the kernel's check runs at signing with the output in hand (kind B), and the admission row records the raw `delivery_observed_digest` and `receipt_delivery_observed_commitment`. Recovery and replay compare the receipt's value with that column and the retained output with `delivery_observed_digest`.
  - Projection holders (Task 5, Task 10: the active-response coordinator, the control-plane security store, `verify_response_simulation_receipt`, the security event consumer): the first verification opens the projection and content commitments, and the holder records the verified values (`verified_projection_commitments`) beside the body, report or event in its own row. Re-verification during recovery, re-projection or event replay compares the receipt's values with them.
  - Compacted replay's content recompute stays optional: it runs only when `opening_status` is `Present` and is skipped otherwise.

A missing opening, a mismatch or an unknown key refuses. `scripts/check-response-lifecycle.py` and `scripts/tests/check-durable-lifecycle.test.py` change with it, and the test file gains refusal cases for a missing content opening, a tampered projection and a `body` that does not open the content commitment.

### Task 12: F commits 18 to 21, `HttpReceipt` deletion and the HTTP middleware SDKs

**Interfaces:**
- `chio-http-core` (Root-held `receipt.rs`, D17 ACK): `HttpReceipt`, `HttpReceiptBody`, `HttpReceipt::sign`, the `ChioReceipt` projection, `caller_identity_hash`, `HttpAuthority::finalize_receipt`, `finalize_decision_receipt`, `sign_transport_deny_receipt` and `TransportDenyInput` are deleted; every HTTP receipt is signed by `sign_decision_receipt` or `sign_final_receipt` below.
  ```rust
  pub struct HttpCallPreimages {
      /// `{"body": <HttpBody>, "caller": <HttpCaller>, "method": .., "request_id": .., "route": <route pattern>}`
      pub request: serde_json::Value,
      /// `{"body": <HttpBody>, "status": <u16>}`; `None` before the upstream answers.
      pub response: Option<serde_json::Value>,
  }
  /// The body is always committed as the raw bytes the upstream received or returned, tagged
  /// (unambiguous by tag): `{"kind":"bytes","content_type":<string or null>,"b64u":<unpadded base64url>}`
  /// or `{"kind":"absent"}`. No JSON parsing, so the preimage is unambiguous and WYSIWYS holds.
  /// `absent` means the upstream received or returned no body, which is the response form of
  /// every deny, refusal and bad-gateway path where the upstream never answered. A body the
  /// middleware refused to read or deliver (a request or response body over its limit, or a
  /// refused event stream) is `{"kind":"refused","limit":<u64>}`, and a response body whose read
  /// failed or missed its collection deadline is `{"kind":"read_failed","bytes_read":<u64>}`,
  /// where `bytes_read` counts the bytes received before the failure or the deadline. In both,
  /// no byte of the body is committed, and the preimage states that it was not read in full.
  /// The caller the evaluator resolved, committed inside the request preimage with every
  /// `CallerIdentity` member: `{"kind":"authenticated","auth_method":<AuthMethod>,
  /// "subject":<CallerIdentity.subject>,"verified":<bool>,"tenant":<string or null>,
  /// "agent_id":<string or null>}` or `{"kind":"anonymous","tenant":<string or null>,
  /// "agent_id":<string or null>}`. `subject` is the identifier the credential carried, or the
  /// `esk1.` keyed string (P-g) when it is an enterprise principal. No unkeyed digest of the
  /// identity is ever stored or signed.
  impl HttpAuthority {
      pub fn sign_decision_receipt(&self, draft: ReceiptDraft, preimages: &HttpCallPreimages, rng: &dyn Rng) -> Result<SignedReceipt>;
      pub fn sign_final_receipt(&self, draft: ReceiptDraft, preimages: &HttpCallPreimages, rng: &dyn Rng) -> Result<SignedReceipt>;
  }
  ```
  `tool_server = "http"`, `tool_name = "<METHOD> <route_pattern>"`. The decision receipt commits the empty output; the final receipt commits the response object.
  **Caller binding.** `caller_identity_hash` is deleted and `authority` is `None` until M3, so the parameter commitment is the only signed carrier of which caller was evaluated. The request preimage therefore includes the `caller` object above, taken from the `CallerIdentity` the evaluator authenticated (never from a header or body the caller supplied). The preimage is committed under the `chio.receipt.parameters.v1\0` domain with a salt drawn from the `Rng` seam, so the identity is hidden from a receipt holder and an unkeyed identity digest appears nowhere; this is the same salted-commitment rule as the body, and it adds no purpose label to the tenant index key set. An enterprise subject appears in its keyed `esk1.` form from P-g, never as the raw principal. The decision receipt and the final receipt for one call commit the same `caller` object. A caller-holder who opens the commitment sees only their own identity. The `tenant` and `agent_id` members record the tenant and agent context the evaluator saw, so two evaluations that differ only there commit differently; tenant scoping of the receipt itself still comes from `tenant_binding`.
  **Authorization projection.** `HttpKernelAuthorizationRequest` (`authority_projection.rs`) is the requested-arguments object the kernel evaluates against the capability before any receipt exists. Its `caller_identity_hash` member is replaced by `caller`, the same caller object the request preimage commits, and `capability_binding` takes that object instead of a hash. Capability evaluation therefore stays bound to the authenticated caller, the kernel's own receipt for that evaluation commits the object under its parameter commitment, and no unkeyed identity digest remains. No capability or policy names `caller_identity_hash` today, so no scope changes meaning.
  **Entry points.** Every HTTP receipt producer on `main`, with its replacement and preimages. The request preimage is always the full object above; a producer that signs a final receipt keeps the request preimage it built for the decision, because a signed decision receipt carries commitments, not preimages.

  | Entry point on `main` | Callers | After F | Request body | Response preimage |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `HttpAuthority::sign_decision_receipt` | `chio-tower` with a durable sink; `api protect` evaluate | `sign_decision_receipt` | buffered bytes (tower, proxy); the evaluate request's `body` (sidecar) | none (empty output) |
  | `HttpAuthority::finalize_receipt` | `chio-tower` after the inner service answers, and on deny | `sign_final_receipt` with the request preimage `prepare` built | buffered bytes | forwarded response: the upstream status and the buffered bytes, or a refusal form (Tower response body, below); deny: `{"body":{"kind":"absent"},"status":<deny status>}` |
  | `HttpAuthority::sign_transport_deny_receipt` | `chio-tower` `build_payload_too_large_response` (the body exceeds `max_body_bytes`, before buffering) | `sign_final_receipt`; caller from the identity extractor, which runs before buffering; `request_id` minted as today | `{"kind":"refused","limit":<max_body_bytes>}` | `{"body":{"kind":"absent"},"status":413}` |
  | `HttpAuthority::finalize_decision_receipt` | `api protect` proxy `finalize_and_record_receipt`: forwarded response, deny, refusal status, bad gateway | `sign_final_receipt` with the request preimage the proxy built in `evaluate` | buffered bytes | forwarded: the upstream status and the bytes `router.rs` already buffers (`resp.body()`); upstream body over the limit or failed while collected: the refusal forms below; deny, refusal and bad gateway before any upstream response: `{"body":{"kind":"absent"},"status":<status>}` |
  | `chio-api-protect` `proxy/receipts.rs` `build_manual_receipt` | `revoked_proxy_response`, `revoked_sidecar_evaluate_response` | proxy: `sign_final_receipt`; sidecar: `sign_decision_receipt` | proxy: buffered bytes; sidecar: the evaluate request's `body` | proxy: `{"body":{"kind":"absent"},"status":403}`; sidecar: none |
  | `sidecar_submit_receipt_handler` (`HttpReceipt::sign`) | operator job observations on `POST /v1/receipts` | `sign_decision_receipt`; `request_id` `job-receipt-submission:<job_uid>`, route `/v1/receipts`, caller anonymous | the submitted bytes | none (the response carries only the receipt id) |

  **Sidecar request body (G3).** The sidecar holds only the body digest today: `ChioHttpRequest` carries `body_hash` and `body_length`. Under G3 it receives the preimage over its existing channel, so `ChioHttpRequest` (with `spec/schemas/chio-http/v1/chio-http-request.schema.json` and `evaluate-request.schema.json`) replaces `body_hash` and `body_length` with `body: HttpBody`, every SDK middleware sends the tagged body, and an evaluate request without `body` refuses at decode. The body is bounded by the limit the proxy already applies to request bodies, `input::MAX_BODY_BYTES` (10 MiB, `chio-api-protect/src/proxy/input.rs:6`), and the evaluate request's read limit (`sidecar.rs:10`) covers the base64 form of a body at that bound. A middleware holding a larger body sends `{"kind":"refused","limit":<limit>}`, and the sidecar denies with a refusal receipt (guard `chio_api_protect_request_body_limit_guard`). The openings of an HTTP receipt therefore never carry or depend on an unsalted body digest.
- Wrapper registry: the same commit that deletes `HttpReceipt` removes `chio.http-receipt.v1` from the wrapper schema registry (`spec/schemas/registry.json`, the core-types wrapper id table and `KNOWN_SIGNED_ARTIFACT_SCHEMAS`), so no merged state has the id without the type.
- `chio-api-protect`: `/v1/evaluate` returns `{"receipt": <chio.receipt.v1>}` and no openings in M1 (P2-3: spec 7.7 lists no such surface, and the caller copy is an M2 cut). In mediated mode the openings persist with the append (Task 4); in sidecar mode without a store they are dropped. `spec/schemas/chio-http/v1/evaluate-response.schema.json` changes with it.
- `chio-tower` keeps the request preimage `prepare` builds from its buffered request body, signs the deny and 413 paths as the entry-point table says, and attaches the receipt from the `SignedReceipt` (the openings are dropped unless a durable sink persists them with the append).
  **Tower response body (decided).** Tower buffers the inner service's response body before it signs the final receipt and returns the response. `ChioService` and `ChioLayer` gain `max_response_body_bytes`, default `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (8 MiB, `chio-tower/src/service.rs:20`, the existing request-body limit), set with `with_max_response_body_bytes`. Collection also has a deadline. `ChioService` and `ChioLayer` gain `request_timeout: Option<Duration>` (unset by default; `with_request_timeout`), which bounds the whole call from the moment `call` starts, and `response_body_collect_timeout: Duration`, default `DEFAULT_RESPONSE_BODY_COLLECT_TIMEOUT` = 30 s (a new constant beside `DEFAULT_MAX_BODY_BYTES` in `chio-tower/src/service.rs`; `with_response_body_collect_timeout`). With a request timeout, collection runs within the request's remaining budget: the deadline is the call's start plus `request_timeout`. Without one, the deadline is `response_body_collect_timeout` after collection starts. The deadline is a `tokio::time::timeout_at` over the collection future, measured on the tokio timer (which tests pause), never on `SystemTime` or the authority clock, and it appears in no signed field.
  - Under the limit: the final receipt commits the raw bytes in the OQ7 form, and Tower returns the buffered body with the receipt attached, as today.
  - Over the limit: Tower stops reading, returns 502 with no byte of the upstream body, and signs a refusal receipt: decision `Deny` with guard `chio_tower_response_body_limit_guard`, response preimage `{"body":{"kind":"refused","limit":<max_response_body_bytes>},"status":502}`.
  - Body-read error: Tower returns 502 and signs a refusal receipt with guard `chio_tower_response_body_read_guard` and response preimage `{"body":{"kind":"read_failed","bytes_read":<u64>},"status":502}`.
  - Deadline missed: a body that stops yielding frames before it ends (for example an `application/octet-stream` body that sends one small frame and then stays pending) cannot hold the call open past the deadline. Tower stops collecting, returns 502 with no byte of the upstream body, and signs a refusal receipt with guard `chio_tower_response_body_deadline_guard` and response preimage `{"body":{"kind":"read_failed","bytes_read":<bytes received before the deadline>},"status":502}`. The guard tells a deadline refusal from a read error; the body tag is the same closed `read_failed` form.
  - Decision record and cancellation: collection starts after the kernel evaluation and, with a durable sink, after the decision receipt is signed and appended (`service.rs:184-192`). A refusal for limit, read error or deadline changes neither: the decision receipt and the kernel's authorization record stay as signed and appended, and the refusal receipt is the call's final receipt, carrying the decision receipt id in its final metadata like every final receipt and appended through `persist_http_receipt` (an append failure returns 502 without a receipt, as today). Cancellation is unchanged: when the caller drops the request future during collection, collection stops with it and no final receipt is signed, as when the future is dropped during `inner.call` today; with a durable sink the decision receipt is the record of the call.
  - Streaming: no route behind `chio-tower` or the `api protect` proxy streams a body past the receipt layer, and no route on `main` needs to. A route that needs streaming is named in this plan with its receipt semantics before it may stream. Until then Tower refuses a `text/event-stream` response before reading it, because an event stream has no end to buffer to: 502, guard `chio_tower_response_stream_guard`, response preimage `{"body":{"kind":"refused","limit":<max_response_body_bytes>},"status":502}`.
  - The `api protect` proxy already collects the upstream body under the egress contract's `max_response_bytes` (`DEFAULT_UPSTREAM_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES`, 64 MiB, `chio-api-protect/src/spec_discovery.rs:12`) and within its request timeout, so it has no streaming mode and needs no separate collection knob. `upstream_request_timeout` (default `DEFAULT_UPSTREAM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` = 20 s, `chio-api-protect/src/proxy/config.rs:8`) is set with `ClientBuilder::timeout` (`proxy/state.rs:548`), reqwest's total-request timeout, which runs from connect until the body ends; body collection therefore always runs within the remaining budget of that timeout. Mapping, on the typed egress errors below: `HttpEgressError::ResponseTooLarge { max, .. }` becomes the `refused` form with `limit` = `max` (guard `chio_api_protect_upstream_response_limit_guard`); `BodyCollection { kind: Timeout, bytes_collected, .. }` becomes `{"kind":"read_failed","bytes_read":<bytes_collected>}` with guard `chio_api_protect_upstream_response_deadline_guard`; `BodyCollection { kind: Other, bytes_collected, .. }` becomes the same form with guard `chio_api_protect_upstream_response_read_guard`; `Transport { .. }` and every contract refusal (`RedirectLimitExceeded`, `DnsResolutionFailed`, the scheme, authority and network denials) happen before any response and stay `absent` (bad gateway, as today). The decision receipt and cancellation behave as for Tower.
  - Egress error contract (`crates/protocol/chio-egress-contract`). On `main`, `collect_capped_response` (`src/reqwest_helper.rs:434-466`) counts `observed` locally and returns a chunk failure through `map_reqwest_error`, which drops the count, and the pre-response `execute` failure (`:212`) returns the same `HttpEgressError::Transport(RequestFailure)`, so a caller cannot tell the two phases apart or learn the bytes collected. The error type changes in place, with no compat overload and no second function: `Transport(#[from] RequestFailure)` becomes `Transport { kind: TransportFailureKind, source: RequestFailure }`, a failure before the response head arrives (the `execute` call, the redirect `Location` parse at `:228-229` and the redirect request build at `:384`), and a new variant `BodyCollection { kind: TransportFailureKind, bytes_collected: u64, source: RequestFailure }` is a failure after the head, while `collect_capped_response` reads chunks, with `bytes_collected` the body bytes received before it. `TransportFailureKind` is the closed enum `Timeout | Other`, set from `reqwest::Error::is_timeout()`. `map_reqwest_error` is replaced by `pre_response_failure(error)` and `body_collection_failure(error, bytes_collected)`, and the `#[from]` conversion is removed, so every construction names its phase. Both variants keep the display `urn:chio:error:transport:http-failed`, so `spec/errors/registry.yaml` is unchanged; `ResponseTooLarge` keeps its shape. Callers of `send_with_contract` on `main` (`git grep -n "send_with_contract(" origin/main -- crates`): `chio-anchor` `src/evm/rpc.rs:80`; `chio-link` `src/chainlink.rs:72`, `src/pyth.rs:101`; `chio-settle` `src/evm/decode.rs:173`, `src/observe.rs:344`; `chio-external-guards` `src/external/http_egress.rs:69`; `chio-guard-registry` `src/oci.rs:818`; `chio-siem` `src/alerting.rs:350,602` and the exporters `datadog.rs:274`, `elastic.rs:213`, `ocsf_exporter.rs:258`, `splunk.rs:202`, `sumo_logic.rs:253`, `webhook.rs:337`; `chio-api-protect` `src/proxy/router.rs:337`, `src/spec_discovery.rs:76`; `chio-cli` `src/cli/dispatch/proof/env.rs:1023`; `chio-finding-market-canary` `src/main.rs:188`; `chio-proof-room` `src/lib.rs:618`; `chio-mcp-remote` `src/remote_mcp/oauth/bearer_auth.rs:630`, `src/remote_mcp/session_identity.rs:137`; and the crate's own tests (`src/tests.rs`, eight sites, and `src/tests/single_attempt.rs:94`, whose `Transport(_)` match becomes `Transport { kind: TransportFailureKind::Other, .. }`). Only the `api protect` proxy branches on the phase; every other caller formats or wraps the error and matches no variant (no `HttpEgressError::Transport` match exists outside the crate's tests), so each compiles unchanged in behavior and the workspace check in Verification covers them.
- Readers: `chio-bundle-verify` loses `verify_http_receipt_evidence` (item 6 part 6a moved `evidence_export/verification.rs` there and deleted the control-plane file; this PR edits the extracted verifier only); `chio-store-sqlite` `retained_read.rs` and `receipt_query_snapshot/export/metadata.rs` drop the HTTP branch.
- SDK middleware verifies the receipt with its language's shared verifier (Task 17): Python (`chio_sdk`, `chio-asgi`, `chio-django`, `chio-fastapi`, `chio-streaming`, `chio-langchain` tests), JVM (`chio-sdk-jvm`, `chio-spring-boot`), .NET (`ChioMiddleware`), TypeScript (`node-http`, `express`, `fastify`, `elysia`, `ai-sdk`, `chio-ai-sdk-middleware`, `conformance`), Go (`chio-go-http`), the ten `examples/hello-*/smoke.sh` and `scripts/tests/check-receipt-query-http.test.py`.
- SDK middleware sends the tagged request body to the sidecar instead of `body_hash` and `body_length` (Sidecar request body above), refusing locally with the `refused` form over `input::MAX_BODY_BYTES`. Files on `main` that build the evaluate request today: Python `chio-sdk-python/src/chio_sdk/{client.py,models_supplemental.py,testing.py}`, `chio-asgi/src/chio_asgi/middleware.py`, `chio-django/src/chio_django/middleware.py`, `chio-fastapi/src/chio_fastapi/decorators.py`, `chio-streaming/src/chio_streaming/{middleware.py,eventbridge.py,flink.py,nats.py,pubsub.py,pulsar.py,redis_streams.py}` (which forward the message body for the first time); JVM `chio-sdk-jvm/src/main/kotlin/world/chio/sdk/{ChioClient.kt,ChioTypes.kt}`, `chio-spring-boot/src/main/kotlin/world/chio/ChioFilter.kt`, `chio-streaming-flink/src/main/kotlin/world/chio/flink/DefaultParametersExtractor.kt`; .NET `ChioMiddleware/src/{ChioMiddlewareExtensions.cs,ChioTypes.cs}`; TypeScript `node-http/src/{interceptor.ts,types.ts}`, `fastify/src/plugin.ts`, `elysia/src/plugin.ts`, `ai-sdk/src/client.ts`, `conformance/src/verify.ts`; Go `chio-go-http/{helpers.go,types.go,types_helpers.go}`; C++ `chio-cpp/include/chio/http_substrate.hpp`, `chio-cpp/src/http_substrate.cpp`, `chio-drogon/src/drogon.cpp`; `examples/hello-drogon/smoke.sh`. Each package gains `sends_tagged_body` and `refuses_body_over_limit` in its own test idiom.

## Amendment A1: value-carrying metadata fields, guard error text and the cage projection

Status: proposed amendment to the merged plan, written against Root-2's decisions for #1727 (`root2/decision-1727-metadata-split-and-projections.md`) and the lead's #1727 review. **F stays stopped until this amendment merges.** No F commit that switches on metadata refusal (Task 8) or signs a block below in a new form lands before it.

### Rules (Root-2 decision 2)

- **Salted commitment is the default** for every field that carries a caller or tool value, or an unsalted digest whose preimage is low-entropy or contains such a value.
- A field stays **plaintext only in a minimal typed projection**, only when a **named downstream reader** needs it in plaintext, and only when the value is a closed code, or is entropy-backed (drawn from the `Rng` seam) or keyed (an operator key). The registry records it `CommittedSafe` with that reader and a Safe reason. A deterministic id or digest whose preimage holds a caller, provider or low-entropy value is committed even when Chio computes it. No blanket block-level Safe classification.
- **Derived digests and copies follow their inputs.** A retained plaintext digest whose preimage includes a committed field is itself committed or keyed, and a committed value is never copied into another plaintext key. `digest_aliases.py` (below) censuses every digest-named receipt metadata field, every carrier of the governed intent hash and every read of a committed source value in the receipt producers; each line has a rule stating its preimage and decision.
- **Fields with no reader are committed** (OQ-A1-1, Root-2), including closed codes and Chio ids.
- **No silent drop and no fallback.** A removed field is either committed or replaced by a named projection field; a reader that needs a value it can no longer read refuses with a named error. A present block that does not decode refuses (for `financial`, `MalformedFinancialMetadata`) and is never read as absent. Unregistered keys stay refused (P-d); that holds for hook metadata only once the reserved-key PR adds the check to the hook merge at `runtime_admission.rs:48`.
- **Entropy failure denies:** a salt draw failure refuses signing (Task 2 `RngError`), for metadata salts as for parameter and content salts.
- **Raw evidence and openings stay private under disclosure authority.** Salts live in `receipt_openings`; operator-readable plaintext lives in `receipt_metadata_values`; both are read only by the operator's own store and by callers with the `receipt-openings:read` credential scope (Task 4). Neither is published beside a public commitment, except a salt-only opening in a carrier that already holds the complete preimage (OQ9 wording).

### Mechanism

- **No whole-block commitments.** Every block is split by field; each committed field has its own salt and `salt_ref`. The rows below follow the lead's #1727 review addendum (b) and were checked against the census.
- `MetadataCommitment { alg: CommitmentAlg, value: Sha256Digest, salt_ref: SaltRef }` (the receipt commitment shape), `salt_ref` `metadata.<block>.<field>` (#1706 grammar; a camelCase key uses its snake_case form, e.g. `metadata.wall_guard_outcome.reason`; every `salt_ref` below is at most 64 bytes), domain `chio.receipt.metadata.v1\0 || utf8(salt_ref) || 0x00`, so a metadata opening cannot stand in for a parameter, content or other metadata opening. Preimage: the canonical JSON of the field value. The salt is drawn with the receipt's other salts and refused when equal to any of them.
- The salt goes to `receipt_openings` and the plaintext value to the new erasable `receipt_metadata_values` table, both in the receipt's append transaction (Task 4), replicated with the receipt and erased together (one transaction, tombstones on the openings stream). Both reach the store on `SignedReceipt.openings.metadata` (Task 6): the writer or projection that builds a `MetadataCommitment` returns its `MetadataOpening` (salt and canonical value), `ReceiptDraft.metadata_openings` carries them to the signer, and the signer refuses a `metadata.*` commitment in the draft without exactly one opening (`UnopenedCommitment { salt_ref }`), an opening without its commitment (`OrphanOpening { salt_ref }`) and an opening that does not recompute (`MetadataCommitmentMismatch { salt_ref }`), then returns every opening in `SignedReceipt.openings`. The local append, the remote append request, the admission row, receipt deltas and snapshots carry them unchanged, so no store derives a value it was not handed. Tests `metadata_commitment_without_opening_refuses_signing`, `metadata_opening_without_commitment_refuses_signing`, `metadata_opening_mismatch_refuses_signing`, `append_writes_metadata_values_atomically`, `remote_append_carries_metadata_openings_and_tenant`.
- Openings: an operator store reader opens with its own rows; a remote reader uses `POST /v1/internal/receipts/openings/read` with `receipt-openings:read`; an in-process reader uses `SignedReceipt.openings` at signing; an external verifier opens only with a carried opening under OQ9. A replay, recovery, re-projection or ledger replay check opens nothing: it compares commitment values recorded at first execution (Task 4 re-run rule).
- Refusal vocabulary: `OpeningUnavailable { Erased | Unknown }`, `MetadataCommitmentMismatch { salt_ref }`, `ReceiptMetadataUnavailable { block, field, state: Erased | Missing }`. **Every erased or missing value refuses.** A reader that needs a committed value whose values row or opening is erased or missing returns `ReceiptMetadataUnavailable` for that receipt; it never substitutes a default, an empty value or a signed field. A report over many receipts returns that refusal as the row for the affected receipt and does not render the field.
- **Derived copies are ephemeral.** No store persists a plaintext copy derived from a values row. The claim log's authorization details (`claim_log/metadata.rs:186-225`, `claim_log/authorization.rs:223,307,410`) stop persisting `purpose`, the payment token and the asserted call chain: the persisted claim-log row keeps the `salt_ref`s, and the plaintext details are materialized on read from the values rows, under the same access as the values table (the operator's own store, or the internal read with `receipt-openings:read`). Erasure of the values row therefore leaves nothing to read, and the next read refuses. Replication carries values rows and their tombstones, never a materialized copy. Test `derived_details_follow_values_erasure`: append, read (details present), erase, read (refuses), replica import of the erased state (refuses), replica import before erasure followed by the tombstone (refuses).

`receipt_metadata_values` DDL, probed with `/usr/bin/sqlite3` 3.51.0 (13 of 13 cases as expected; `docs/superpowers/plans/m1-item2-census/metadata-blocks/values-ddl.sql`, `values_probe.py`, `values-probe-result.json`, sha256 `c4a6295c49fdb9401b833bd7c2347a8add05f15e64993d16d6c7bef98428785f`):

```sql
CREATE TABLE receipt_metadata_values (
  receipt_id TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(receipt_id) = 'text' AND instr(receipt_id, char(0)) = 0 AND length(receipt_id) = 64 AND receipt_id NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'),
  salt_ref TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(salt_ref) = 'text' AND instr(salt_ref, char(0)) = 0 AND length(salt_ref) BETWEEN 10 AND 64 AND salt_ref GLOB 'metadata.[a-z]*' AND salt_ref NOT GLOB '*[^a-z0-9_.]*' AND salt_ref NOT GLOB '*..*' AND salt_ref NOT GLOB '*.'),
  state TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(state) = 'text' AND instr(state, char(0)) = 0 AND state IN ('present', 'erased')),
  value TEXT,
  state_at INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(state_at) = 'integer' AND state_at >= 0),
  CHECK ((state = 'present' AND typeof(value) = 'text' AND json_valid(value)) OR (state = 'erased' AND value IS NULL)),
  PRIMARY KEY (receipt_id, salt_ref)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
```

### Census

`docs/superpowers/plans/m1-item2-census/metadata-blocks/metadata_blocks.py` lists, at `bc5579aaf4`, every non-test line under `crates` and `sdks` naming one of 31 block keys by literal, by a constant whose value is the key, or by a typed accessor (`financial_metadata()`, `governed_transaction_metadata()`, `delivery_contract()`), with its role (writer, reader, constant, other). A test line is one under a test path or inside an item gated by a test-only cfg, `#[cfg(test)]` or `#[cfg(all(..))]` with `test` as a top-level argument (a module, function, impl or field), including an item whose cfg attribute is followed by further attributes spanning several lines. A cfg that also compiles outside tests (`any(test, ..)`, `not(test)`, a feature gate) never marks a test line, so a production reader behind it stays in the census. Every reader and writer line matches a rule in `rules.tsv` whose role column is its own role or `any`, so a writer catch-all never classifies a new reader. It fails closed: exit 2 on a git failure, exit 1 on a non-commit revision, a malformed rule, an empty block or an unclassified reader or writer; `--self-check` requires a nonexistent revision and an empty rule set to exit nonzero, a multiline-attribute test module to be excluded, an `all(test, ..)` item to be excluded while `any(test, ..)`, `not(test)` and feature-gated items stay counted, and a reader that matches only a writer rule to stay unclassified (all hold). Output `blocks-bc5579aaf4.tsv`: 320 lines, 31 keys, 0 unclassified. Lines whose literal is unrelated (a provider `stream` flag, the `otel` cargo feature, the `acp` payment adapter) are classified `unrelated` by rule. Field-level readers reached after a typed decode are named in the table from a direct grep of the field. The settlement readers of #1705 (head `afe49dcd0e`) and #1710 (head `cbd953833f`) are listed at those heads. The `rules.tsv` notes state the decided per-field form of each row.

`docs/superpowers/plans/m1-item2-census/metadata-blocks/digest_aliases.py` censuses derived digests and copies at `bc5579aaf4`. It lists every non-test line of three kinds:

- `field`: a digest-named field of a signed receipt metadata type under `chio-core-types/src/receipt/`.
- `intent`: a struct field or JSON key carrying the governed intent binding hash.
- `copy`: a read of a committed source value in the receipt producer files.

Test lines are excluded as in `metadata_blocks.py`, whose `cfg_test_lines` it reuses. Each line must match a rule in `digest-rules.tsv` stating its preimage, whether the preimage holds a committed field, and the decision. The script fails closed the same way: exit 2 on a git failure, exit 1 on a non-commit revision, a kind with no lines, or an unclassified line. Its `--self-check` refuses a nonexistent revision and an empty rule set. Output `digests-bc5579aaf4.tsv`: 170 lines (61 field, 78 intent, 31 copy), 0 unclassified. The executing lane reruns both scripts at its own base before P-d part 2 and before F, and every new line gets a rule.

### Per-field table

Columns: the field; its producer; every reader and whether it needs plaintext; the retained projection (plaintext, with the named reader and Safe reason) or the commitment; the opening path; the refusal when the value or opening is missing or wrong. "Store" means the operator's own `receipt_openings` and `receipt_metadata_values` rows.

**`financial`** (writers: kernel responses, validation and evaluation, 31 census lines incl. `terminal.rs:1422`, `reconciliation.rs:398-403`, `tool_output.rs:521,577`, `finding_verified_fix.rs:1342`, `iou_store.rs:397`)

| Field | Readers (plaintext need) | Projection or commitment | Opening | Refusal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cost_charged`, `currency` | settlement: `settlement_observer.rs:113,258-300` (#1705, #1710), `settlement_ledger.rs:46-100` (#1710), `reconciliation.rs:352-370` (#1710); credit `local_account.rs:165`, `receivable_iou.rs:775`; `channel/state.rs:359`, `channel_terminal.rs:579`, `participant_evidence.rs:577`, `remote_projection.rs:896`; claim log `claim_log/metadata.rs:156,175`, `schema.rs:74`; SIEM `event.rs:96`, `sink_projection.rs:127`; finding `standing.rs:191`, `cost.rs:87,132`; `authoritative_spend.rs:39` (all need plaintext) | **Settlement projection**, `CommittedSafe`: the kernel-computed charge settlement must reconcile against the payment rail; not a tool value | none | unchanged reader errors (`settlement_observer.rs:267-300`) |
| `settlement_status`, `payment_reference` | `settlement_observer.rs:305-337,820-848` (#1705, #1710), `settlement_ledger.rs:46-100,98` (#1710) | **Settlement projection**, `CommittedSafe`: kernel settlement state and the rail transaction id (`hold.reserved_payment_reference`), read by settlement and the ledger transcript | none | unchanged |
| Retained-payment shape on a denied receipt: `payment_authorization_ambiguous`, `payment_authorization_error_code`, `payment_attempt_reference` (writers `async_evaluation_core.rs:1550-1560`, `nested_flow_evaluation.rs:1225-1227`) | `retained_payment_settlement`, `settlement_observer.rs:113-145` (#1705, #1710), which requires the attempt reference | **Settlement projection**, `CommittedSafe`, as the `RetainedAmbiguous` variant below. The error code is a closed code (`unavailable`, `rail_error`, `declined`, `insufficient_funds`, from `async_evaluation_core.rs:1477-1482`). The attempt reference is `payment_operation_reference` (`dispatch.rs:1043-1052`): the durable operation id for an operation with a payment requirement, else the request id. F requires the durable operation id on this path (a payment authorization only runs for an operation with a payment requirement) and refuses signing otherwise; the id-entropy test covers it | none | missing attempt reference refuses as today (`"retained payment requires a payment attempt reference"`); an undecodable block refuses `MalformedFinancialMetadata` |
| `grant_index`, `budget_remaining`, `budget_total`, `delegation_depth`, `root_budget_holder`, `attempted_cost`, `oracle_evidence` | credit, channel, projection, claim log and finding readers above (budget and grant accounting) | **Accounting projection**, `CommittedSafe` per field with its readers: kernel-computed budget, grant and conversion state | none | unchanged |
| `cost_breakdown`, split at both tool-value writers. `tool_output.rs:521-527` merges the tool-reported `cost.breakdown` with the kernel `payment_breakdown` and the cross-currency note; `reconciliation.rs:398` writes the tool-reported `realized_cost.breakdown` (`reconciliation.rs:152`) alone | kernel recovery check `terminal/financial_receipt.rs:18,118` compares it with `payment_cost_breakdown(..)`; `terminal.rs:521-527` copies the persisted block for byte-exact replay; `effect_obligation/preparation.rs:307-320` copies kept fields | `cost_breakdown` keeps only kernel content (the payment breakdown and the cross-currency note): **Projection**, `CommittedSafe` with those readers. The tool-reported breakdown from either writer moves to `reported_cost_breakdown`, a **commitment** `metadata.financial.reported_cost_breakdown`; a reconciled receipt has no kernel breakdown, so its `cost_breakdown` is absent | the recovery check compares kernel-derived values only and needs no opening. The journal path that recovery checks never carries the cross-currency note (the note arises only on a metered or oracle conversion); F pins that with a test, so a recovered receipt with a note mismatches and refuses as today. The operator opens the reported part from its values row | recovery mismatch refuses as today; an erased reported part refuses its reader with `ReceiptMetadataUnavailable` |

The settlement readers move from `metadata.get("financial")` field picks to a typed enum read by a new decoder, `financial_settlement_projection()`, not by `financial_metadata()` (which decodes `FinancialReceiptMetadata` and cannot decode the partial denied block):

```rust
pub enum FinancialSettlementProjection {
    Charged { cost_charged: u64, currency: String, settlement_status: SettlementStatus, payment_reference: Option<String> },
    RetainedAmbiguous { payment_authorization_error_code: PaymentAuthorizationErrorCode, payment_attempt_reference: String },
}
```

The decoder checks the whole block before it builds the projection. It first decodes the complete block into one strict closed type, `FinancialBlock`, with two variants, each `deny_unknown_fields`: `Charged`, which is `FinancialReceiptMetadata` in its F form (the accounting projection `grant_index`, `budget_remaining`, `budget_total`, `delegation_depth`, `root_budget_holder`, `attempted_cost`, `oracle_evidence`; the settlement fields; the kernel `cost_breakdown`; the `reported_cost_breakdown` commitment), and `RetainedAmbiguous`, exactly `{payment_authorization_ambiguous: true, payment_authorization_error_code, payment_attempt_reference}` (`async_evaluation_core.rs:1553-1560`). It then derives `FinancialSettlementProjection` from the decoded variant. It returns `Ok(None)` only when the receipt has no `financial` key, and `Err(MalformedFinancialMetadata)` for a present block that decodes as neither variant (an unknown member, a missing member or a mixed shape). Readers never read `cost_breakdown` or `reported_cost_breakdown`. Tests `charged_receipt_with_accounting_fields_yields_charged` (an ordinary charged block with `grant_index`, the budget fields, `delegation_depth`, `root_budget_holder` and a kernel `cost_breakdown` decodes to `Charged`) and `financial_block_with_unknown_member_refuses`. Test `ambiguous_denied_receipt_yields_retained_payment`: a denied receipt from the ambiguous authorization path, through #1705's `retained_payment_settlement`, produces a `RetainedPayment` observation after F; a malformed block refuses. `budget_authority` (`hold_id`, `authorize.exposure_units`, read at `settlement_observer.rs:98,138,869-870`) is already `CommittedSafe`; its kept fields are named in the registry reason. #1705 rebases onto the projection type in F (D17); #1710's readers follow the atomic rule below.
