**Payee binding across credit artifacts.** The four credit carriers of an obligation atom carry a salt-only opening `payee_binding_opening` (an `Opening` with `salt_ref` `metadata.governed_transaction.payee_binding` and `payload: null`) next to the atom: the channel advance (`chio-settle` `VerifiedChannelReceiptBindingV1` and the channel receipt binding it is built from), the credit admission record (`CreditExposureReservationRecordV1`, supplied with the atom to `prepare_committed` and `validate_committed_obligation`), the factor claim (`ReceivableClaimVerificationV1`) and the IOU (`ReceivableIouBody`). Each already holds the creditor id and settlement destination in plaintext, so the opening exposes nothing new, and open question 9 allows it (the commitment's preimage, the raw payee digest, is rebuilt from fields the carrier holds). The atom builder takes the opening from the caller copy or from the internal openings read (`POST /v1/internal/receipts/openings/read` with `receipt-openings:read`). The credit facility bind is minted before any receipt exists, so it keeps its raw `payee_binding_digest` beside its plaintext creditor and destination. Every check below rebuilds the raw digest with `derive_obligation_payee_binding_digest` and verifies the atom commitment over it with the carried opening; it refuses with `MetadataCommitmentMismatch { salt_ref: "metadata.governed_transaction.payee_binding" }` exactly when the opened commitment differs from the atom's value and with `ReceiptMetadataUnavailable` exactly when the carrier has no opening, each as its own variant of the crate's error (`ChannelError`, `CreditAdmissionError`, `FactorError`, `ReceivableIouError`), never folded into a generic binding mismatch.

| Call site (origin/main) | Today | Check after F |
|---|---|---|
| `crates/economy/chio-settle/src/channel/state.rs:403-415` | `atom.payee_binding_digest()` equals `derive_channel_payee_binding_digest(payee_id, payee_beneficiary_address)` from the channel intent | verify the atom commitment over that rebuilt digest with the advance's `payee_binding_opening` |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/obligation/credit_admission.rs:1169` (`CreditExposureReservationRecordV1::validate`) | the record's raw digest equals the bind's | unchanged: both sides are the facility bind's raw digest |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/obligation/credit_admission.rs:1365` (`validate_obligation_terms`) | `atom.payee_binding_digest()` equals the record's raw digest | verify the atom commitment over the record's raw digest (the bind's) with the record's `payee_binding_opening` |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/obligation/credit_admission.rs:1638` (`CreditExposureReservation::validate_committed_binding`) | the record's raw digest equals the bind's | unchanged: both sides are the facility bind's raw digest |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/obligation/credit_facility_bind.rs:118,226-232` | the bind derives and revalidates its raw digest from its creditor and destination | unchanged |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/factor/claim.rs:321-346` | rebuilt raw digest from the atom's creditor and destination equals `atom.payee_binding_digest()` | verify the atom commitment over the rebuilt digest with the claim context's `payee_binding_opening` |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/factor/request.rs:379` (`ReceivableClaimV1::validate_against_atom`) | the receivable claim's digest equals the atom's | the receivable claim carries the atom's commitment (`payee_binding: MetadataCommitment`) and compares commitment values; it holds no destination, so it carries no raw digest |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/receivable_iou.rs:740` | the bind's raw digest equals the atom's | verify the atom commitment over the bind's raw digest with the IOU's `payee_binding_opening` |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/receivable_iou.rs:823` (IOU body) | copies the atom's raw digest | copies the atom's commitment and carries `payee_binding_opening` |
| `crates/economy/chio-credit/src/receivable_iou.rs:912,943-948` (`validate_economics`) | receipt digest and a digest rebuilt from the receipt's payee fields equal the atom's | the receipt's and the atom's commitment values are compared; the digest is rebuilt from the atom's creditor and destination and the atom commitment is verified with the IOU's opening; the credit reader still refuses every production receipt as in the `approved_max` row |

Tests, one per off-host check for match, mismatch and missing opening: `channel_advance_verifies_payee_commitment_with_carried_opening`, `channel_advance_refuses_payee_commitment_mismatch`, `channel_advance_refuses_missing_payee_opening`, `credit_admission_verifies_payee_commitment_with_carried_opening`, `credit_admission_refuses_payee_commitment_mismatch`, `credit_admission_refuses_missing_payee_opening`, `factor_claim_verifies_payee_commitment_with_carried_opening`, `factor_claim_refuses_payee_commitment_mismatch`, `factor_claim_refuses_missing_payee_opening`, `iou_verifies_payee_commitment_with_carried_opening`, `iou_refuses_payee_commitment_mismatch`, `iou_refuses_missing_payee_opening`, and `receivable_claim_binds_atom_payee_commitment`; `obligation_atom_validate_checks_commitment_form` pins the form-only `validate`.

**Session anchor lineage (Root-2 #1728 item 2).** No producer writes a session anchor hash: `GovernedCallChainProvenance` carries only `session_anchor_id`, and the parent-anchor lookup (`kernel/mod.rs:849-880`) needs both an id and a hash. Today only the caller-supplied `lineageReferences` fallback can satisfy it. The reserved-key PR deletes that fallback. After that, the persisted-parent branch of `governed_validation.rs:966-990` (`LocalReceiptArtifact::session_anchor_reference`) has no authoritative source and refuses with the existing "could not be verified against authoritative parent lineage" denial. The live-parent branch, which reads the anchor from the in-memory parent session (`session.session_anchor().reference()`), stays the positive control, with a test. A1 claims no persisted-parent producer. Supporting persisted parents needs a typed, kernel-written anchor reference specified and tested on its own; the existing manually signed fallback fixture is deleted, not repaired.

**`governed_transaction_diagnostics`** (writer `receipt_metadata.rs:68-85,609`; readers `receipt_store/reports.rs:65`, `operator_report/authorization_context.rs:322,407`, `behavioral_analysis.rs:102`): `asserted_call_chain` (a copy of `call_chain` when its evidence class is `Asserted`) and both `lineage_references` members (`session_anchor_id`, `receipt_lineage_statement_id`, copies of `call_chain` members, `receipt_metadata.rs:68-85`) are aliases: each location carries the commitment of the `call_chain` member it copies (`metadata.governed_transaction.cc_<member>`), so the diagnostics block draws no salt of its own. The operator reports read the plaintext from the values rows of those `salt_ref`s; missing or erased refuses the report row. The top-level `lineageReferences` fallback in `kernel/mod.rs:869-877` is deleted in the reserved-key PR, so no reader takes lineage from caller metadata.

**`receipt_context`** (kernel writers `return_context.rs:78`, `post_effect.rs:228`, `preparation.rs:149`, `receipt_persistence.rs:177`, `receipt_identity.rs:80`; ACP-Client writers `kernel_signer.rs:532`, `kernel_checker.rs:264`)

| Field | Readers | Projection or commitment | Opening | Refusal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `request_id`, `evaluation_id`, `session_id` | 13 readers: `projection/authorization.rs:62`, `receipt_identity.rs:70`, `allow_responses.rs:890`, `call_evidence.rs:301,389`, `run_evidence/custody.rs:93`, `process_response_verify.rs:212`, `chio-cross-protocol/execution.rs:242`, `completed_result.rs:140`, `chio-trace-validate` `capture.rs:602`, `observation.rs:269`, `acp-proxy/kernel_signer.rs:282` | **Projection**, `CommittedSafe`, with these readers, only for values the id-entropy test shows are drawn from the `Rng` seam (kernel-issued ids); a request id the caller chooses is committed, with its salt drawn at admission like the namespace salt, and the admission row records its value (`receipt_request_id_commitment`); replay and recovery checks compare the receipt's value with that column, and only a reader that needs the plaintext opens it from the store | none for kept fields | a value that differs from the row's refuses with the site's existing binding-mismatch error; a plaintext reader with a missing or erased opening refuses |
| `request_namespace_digest`, and its copy `admission_operation.request_namespace_digest` (`AdmissionReceiptMetadataV1`, `projection.rs:62`) | writers `receipt_identity.rs:63-80` (called from `refusal.rs:262`, `terminal.rs:772,1549`), `refusal.rs:213`, `terminal.rs:1372`, `post_effect.rs:224`; replay and recovery checks `projection.rs:1280`, `refusal.rs:486,508`, `terminal.rs:815`, `compacted_replay.rs:198`, `call_evidence.rs:314,389-390` (each compares with the admission row or binding); the non-durable discharge check `effect_obligation/preparation.rs:55` | **Commitment** `metadata.receipt_context.request_namespace` over the raw digest: one commitment value, referenced from both `receipt_context` and `admission_operation`, with no second copy. `AuthenticatedRequestNamespace::bind` (`admission_operation/identity.rs:158-178`) computes the digest as an unkeyed `domain_separated_digest` over the authenticated tenant id and the coordinator authority id, so a plaintext copy would let a holder who knows the authority id confirm a guessed tenant. See "Request namespace commitment" below | replay and recovery checks compare the receipt's commitment value with the value recorded on the admission row (kind A) and need no opening, so erasure cannot wedge them; only a reader that needs the raw value opens it | a value that differs from the row's refuses with the site's existing binding-mismatch error; an opening-based reader refuses `MetadataCommitmentMismatch` on a mismatch and `OpeningUnavailable` when the opening is missing or erased |
| ACP-Client `authorization_resource` | `acp-proxy/kernel_signer.rs:342` compares it | **Commitment** `metadata.receipt_context.authorization_resource` (from the tool call), shared with `acp.authorizationResource` | proxy opens over the resource in its own authorization request record | `MetadataCommitmentMismatch` refuses the session update |
| ACP-Client `authorization_parameter_hash` and its alias `acp.authorizationParameterHash` (`kernel_signer.rs:521,540`), an unsalted SHA-256 of the canonical ACP-Client parameters (`interceptor.rs:1057`) | none (the admission authorization projection reads `authorization_parameter_hash` from the action parameters, `projection/authorization.rs:106`, never from metadata) | One **commitment** `metadata.acp.authorization_parameter_hash`, referenced from both locations, so no plaintext digest of the arguments survives beside the salted parameter commitment | the proxy holds the raw hash in its own authorization request record; no reader opens it | none |
| ACP-Client form's other members: `tool_call_id`, `authorization_receipt_id`, `authorization_correlation_id`, `authorization_operation` (aliases of the `acp` members) | none | **Commitment**, each sharing the commitment of its `acp` alias (no reader, OQ-A1-1); the ACP-Client form's `request_id` follows the `request_id` row and aliases `acp.authorizationRequestId` | operator values row where the producer persists openings; the ACP-Client proxy drops its openings (Task 1 producer table) | none |

**Request namespace commitment.** The salt of `metadata.receipt_context.request_namespace` is drawn from the `Rng` seam at admission, so every receipt of one operation (allow, terminal, refusal, recovery) carries the same commitment value. In the same admission transaction the admission operation row records the salt (`request_namespace_salt`) and the commitment value (`receipt_namespace_commitment`) beside its other receipt commitments (Task 7 commit 4). The salt follows the admission-row salt rule of the "Salt copies and erasure" table: recovery re-signs with it before publication and never draws a fresh one, it is cleared at the publication acknowledgement, and afterwards it lives only in `receipt_openings`, where `erase_openings` reaches it. The commitment value is not secret and stays on the row. The raw digest stays in private state, where it keys admission identity and replay (admission rows, `channel_terminal.rs`, `economic_cancellation.rs`, `payment_compensation.rs`, `EconomicRequestBindingV1`). The writers put the row's commitment value into `receipt_context` and `admission_operation`: `RefusalReceiptMetadata::into_signed_metadata` (`receipt_identity.rs:63-80`, called from `refusal.rs:262` and `terminal.rs:772,1549`) and the `AdmissionReceiptMetadataV1` builders (`refusal.rs:213`, `terminal.rs:1372`). A receipt with no durable admission (the non-durable post-effect writer, `post_effect.rs:224`) has no row; it draws the namespace salt with its other salts at signing. Every replay and recovery binding check compares the receipt's commitment value with the row's `receipt_namespace_commitment` (kind A), needs no opening and survives erasure: `validate_receipt_projection` (`projection.rs:1280`), `replay_durable_refusal` (`refusal.rs:486` for the `admission_operation` copy and `refusal.rs:508-510` for the `receipt_context` copy), `validate_completed_durable_receipt` (`terminal.rs:815`), `completed_compacted_value_response` (`compacted_replay.rs:198`, so compacted replay keeps Task 7's rule that it never requires an opening), and the CLI process host's call evidence check (`call_evidence.rs:314,389-390`, against the binding's recorded value). The non-durable discharge check `DischargeBinding::validate_receipt` (`effect_obligation/preparation.rs:55`) has no admission row; it runs in process right after signing and opens the commitment from `SignedReceipt.openings` against the namespace it computed (`preparation.rs:100`), refusing `MetadataCommitmentMismatch`. Only a reader that needs the raw namespace value opens the commitment, from the store or `SignedReceipt.openings`; the private rows give the kernel the raw value, so no replay or recovery path does. Comparing a receipt with its own admission row is not a comparison across receipts: the row records the one commitment value of its own operation, which every receipt of that operation carries, and no two operations share a value. No reader compares the commitment value of one operation's receipt with another's. Tests `same_tenant_receipts_carry_distinct_namespace_commitments` (two operations in one tenant yield different commitment values, and neither equals the raw digest), `recovered_receipt_reuses_persisted_namespace_salt` (a receipt re-signed in recovery carries the commitment value of the original and draws no namespace salt) and `compacted_replay_survives_erased_namespace_opening` (erase the namespace opening of a compacted admission, replay: completes with the same receipt, because the check compares commitment values with the row).

| `chunk_hashes` | CLI `process_response_verify.rs:310,331,353-360` | **Dropped from signed metadata** (spec 7.6: per-chunk digests are not written to any signed field). The content commitment already commits the stream preimage | the CLI recomputes the content commitment from its own chunks and opens it with the caller-copy opening (Task 7) | `OpeningUnavailable` or `ContentCommitmentMismatch` fails verification |

The kernel tests assert its absence (`stream_projection_has_no_chunks_expected`) | none | none |

Session lineage reads the anchor from the session store and `governed_transaction.call_chain`, never from this block |

**Governed intent hash (Root-2 #1728 item 1).** `GovernedTransactionIntent::binding_hash` (`governance.rs:1221-1227`) is an unsalted SHA-256 over the whole canonical intent, including `purpose` and the context. A plaintext copy confirms a guessed purpose when the rest of the intent is known. `digest_aliases.py` lists every carrier of it (kind `intent`), and each falls in one class:

| Class | Carriers | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| Signed receipt metadata | `GovernedTransactionReceiptMetadata.intent_hash` (`receipt/governance.rs:110`), `EconomicAuthorizationReceiptMetadata.economic_intent_digest` (`receipt/economics.rs:317`) | One **commitment** `metadata.governed_transaction.intent_hash` over the raw binding hash, referenced from both fields. The salt is drawn at admission and persisted on the admission operation row, so retries and recovery keep it, and so artifacts minted before signing can carry the commitment value |
| Outbound economic artifacts (leave the deployment to creditors, factors or a rail) | `chio-credit` `ObligationAtomInputV1` (`obligation.rs:103`), `CreditExposureReservationRequest` (`credit_admission.rs:833`), `CreditFacilityBindInputV1` (`credit_facility_bind.rs:42`); kernel `GovernedPaymentContext.intent_hash` (`payment/types.rs:135`) passed to payment adapters; `VerifiedGovernedPayeeBinding.economic_intent_digest` (`kernel/mod.rs:675`) | Carry the **commitment value**, never the raw hash. Equality readers (`participant_evidence.rs:591,600,612`, `receivable_iou.rs:882`) compare commitment values, which are equal by construction |
| Lineage and reports | `RequestLineageRecord.intent_hash` (`session/lineage.rs:41`), `GovernedAuthorizationTransactionContext.intent_hash` (`operator_report/authorization_context.rs:46`) | Carry the **commitment value** |
| Authorization protocol artifacts exchanged only among the caller, its approvers and Chio kernels, all of which hold the intent | `GovernedApprovalTokenBody` and `GovernedApprovalToken.governed_intent_hash` (`governance.rs:1306,1321`), `ThresholdApprovalProposalBody` (`:1469`), `ApprovalSetBody` (`:1727`), `CallChainContinuationTokenBody` and token (`:374,407`), `GovernedApprovalRequest` (`ports/approval.rs:53`) | **Raw hash kept**: approvers sign over the intent they see. These artifacts are never embedded in a receipt, an evidence export or an outbound artifact; the receipt keeps only `approval_artifact_digest` (keyed by the approver signature). Test `no_approval_artifact_in_receipt_or_export` |
| Signed security response receipt bodies | `ResponseExecutionDispatchBinding.governed_intent_hash` (`chio-security-types/src/response.rs:537`), carried in the security response state-transition receipt body | The Task 5 security projection carries the **commitment value**; executors (`chio-quarantine/approval.rs:281`, `executor_proof.rs:111`, `chio-secret-broker/provision.rs:1081`) compare the raw hash only in private dispatch state |
| Deployment-internal rows and active-response dispatch (kernel, store and executors of one deployment, private under disclosure authority) | admission operation rows and approval stores (`governed_approval_claim.rs:63`, `governed_approval_replay.rs:43`, `chio-store-sqlite` approval and replay stores), active-response submission, artifact, dispatch and executor bindings (`active_response_*.rs`, `chio-security-types` `ports/dispatch.rs:64`, `ports/outbox.rs:30`, `response.rs:537`), `request_binding.rs:22`, `response_simulation_report.rs:19` | **Raw hash kept** in private state. Any of these that a receipt or export carries goes through its projection (Task 5), whose kept digests the entropy test covers |

Test `intent_hash_has_no_public_alias`: two intents equal except `purpose` (and, separately, except the context) produce receipts, exports and outbound credit and payment artifacts in which no field equals either raw binding hash, and whose commitment values differ.

**`chio_receipt_signing_nonce`** (writers `signing.rs:106,137`; ACP-Client producer): readers `terminal.rs:747,764`, `compacted_replay.rs:241`, `chio-settle/evm/prepare.rs:504`, `chio-web3/settlement.rs:19,685` require `nonce == governed_receipt_id`, so committing it would break them. **Projection**, `CommittedSafe` with those readers. **Producer fix:** the ACP-Client proxy (`acp-proxy/kernel_signer.rs`) today embeds an unsalted content hash in `body.id`; it mints the id from the `Rng` seam like the kernel, with no content hash. A nonce that fails the kernel-form shape check refuses signing.

**`protocol_admission`** (writer `admission_terminal_receipt.rs:833`; no reader): free-text `last_error` and the closed `last_error_code` both become **commitments** (OQ-A1-1, no reader).

**`protocol_refusal`** (writer `session_ops/protocol_refusal.rs:266`; readers `session_certificate_read.rs:410-411`, `acp-proxy/compliance.rs:352,358` read `session_id`): `request_digest.sha256` (over the original wire bytes), `method_sha256` and `target_sha256` become **commitments**; `session_id` and the refusal code stay in the projection. Registered `Unsafe` by P-d until F.

**`admission_operation`** (writers `refusal.rs:245`, `admission_terminal_receipt.rs:834`, `terminal.rs:760,1450`): `request_binding_hash` (over the immutable request hash and the unsalted parameter hash) becomes the **commitment** `metadata.admission_operation.request_binding`. Its salt is drawn once at admission from the `Rng` seam; in the same admission transaction the admission operation row records the salt (`request_binding_salt`) and the commitment value (`receipt_request_binding_commitment`), and the salt follows the admission-row salt rule of the "Salt copies and erasure" table, as for the request namespace (recovery re-signs with it before publication, it is cleared at the publication acknowledgement, and afterwards it lives only in `receipt_openings`). The writers (`AdmissionReceiptMetadataV1` at `refusal.rs:209-214` and `terminal.rs:1368-1379`) put the row's value into the block. Every same-operation binding check compares the receipt's commitment value with the row's `receipt_request_binding_commitment` (kind A), needs no opening and survives erasure: `validate_receipt_projection` (`projection.rs:1281`), `replay_durable_refusal` (`refusal.rs:487`), `validate_completed_durable_receipt` (`terminal.rs:816`), `completed_compacted_value_response` (`compacted_replay.rs:199`) and the CLI process host's call evidence check (`call_evidence.rs:315`, against the binding's recorded value); a different value refuses with the site's existing binding-mismatch error. The other block readers (`remote_projection.rs:1083`, `payload_compaction/eligibility.rs:155`, `finding_purchase_coordinator.rs:1251`) read the kept Chio state, never the binding. Only a reader that needs the raw binding opens the commitment, from the store or `SignedReceipt.openings`; the admission row gives the kernel the raw hash, so no replay or recovery path does. The block is written only for a durable admission, so every receipt that carries it has a row and no non-durable path draws this salt. Every receipt of one operation carries the same value, which is acceptable because those receipts are already linkable through the kept `operation_id` and `request_id`; across operations the values differ. Tests `operations_carry_distinct_request_binding_commitments` (two operations with the same arguments yield different values, and neither equals the raw hash) and `compacted_replay_survives_erased_request_binding_opening` (erase the request binding opening of a compacted admission, replay: completes with the same receipt). `request_namespace_digest` references the `metadata.receipt_context.request_namespace` commitment (the `receipt_context` row), with no second copy. `operation_id`, `projected_state` and the other Chio state stay in the projection for the external readers (`chio-process/lib.rs:636`, `call_evidence.rs:284`, `nonce_evidence.rs:25,53,120`, `completed_result.rs:77,139`, `chio-mini-swe` `model.py:117`, `repository_proof.py:98,154`). Registered `Unsafe` by P-d until F.

**`finding_pool_mutation`** (writer `receipt_persistence.rs:61,68`; reader `finding_pool_ledger.rs:1570,1577`): the block duplicates the action parameters. It is replaced by the parameter commitment (no new metadata field). At mutation time the ledger rebuilds the expected mutation, opens the parameter commitment with `SignedReceipt.openings` in process (census kind E/P-ii) and records the receipt's `receipt_parameter_commitment` and `receipt_content_commitment` in the mutation's `finding_pool_receipt_outbox` row, beside the mutation's plaintext in its own `finding_pool_debits` and `finding_pool_allocations` rows; a mismatch or missing opening refuses the mutation. Ledger replay rebuilds the expected mutation from those rows and compares the outbox receipt's commitment values with the recorded columns, so it never fails with `OpeningUnavailable` after erasure (Task 4 re-run rule).

**`otel`** (writer `chio-otel-receipt-exporter/sink.rs:368,373`; no receipt reader). Every member is committed: each span attribute value as `metadata.otel.attr_<n>` (n is its position in the sorted attribute names), and the trace id and span id as `metadata.otel.trace_id` and `metadata.otel.span_id` (Chio-minted ids with no receipt reader, OQ-A1-1). The operator's trace backend joins on the exported span, which carries the ids itself; it never reads them from the receipt.

**`scimLifecycle`** and the SCIM deprovisioning receipt (writer `config_and_public.rs:1510-1580`; no receipt reader). Every block member is committed as `metadata.scim_lifecycle.<field>`: `providerId`, `providerRecordId`, `principal`, `subjectKey`, `userId`, `userName`, `revokedCapabilityIds`, `entitlements` and `receiptKind` (OQ-A1-1). The same receipt carries aliases of those values outside the block, each of which would confirm a guessed user:

| Field | Today | Decision |
|---|---|---|
| receipt `id` | `"scim-deprovision-" + sha256(provider_id:user_id:now)` (`:1536-1539`) | drawn from the `Rng` seam like every kernel receipt id; any index keyed by the old derived id moves to the stored id (P-g) |
| `capability_id` | `"scim-user:" + user_id` (`:1541`), where `user_id` is the provider's id or `derive_scim_user_id` (`scim_lifecycle.rs:300-304`) | `"scim-user:"` followed by keyed output under the `enterprise-subject` purpose subkey over the provider and user id; no reader parses it (P-g) |
| `policy_hash` | `sha256("chio.scim-lifecycle-delete.v1:" + provider_id + ":" + subject_key)` (`:1527-1533`) | keyed under the `enterprise-subject` purpose subkey over the same preimage (P-g) |
| action parameters and `content_hash` | `providerId`, `userId`, `userName`, `subjectKey`, `revokedCapabilityIds` (`:1514-1526`) | the parameter and content commitments of the main plan |
| `attribution.subject_key` | `derive_enterprise_subject_key`, an unsalted SHA-256 over the provider scope and the canonical principal (`scim_lifecycle.rs:452-461`) | keyed in P-g (the blocking identity-closure precursor below), together with the remote MCP producer and every consumer |

**`mercury`** (writers `chio-mercury-core/receipt_metadata.rs:283,294`; readers `receipt_metadata.rs:311` and the proof package `proof_package.rs:205-256,376-407,547-548,1020-1032`):

| Fields | Readers | Projection or commitment | Opening | Refusal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `schema`, `decision_context.decision_type`, `chronology.stage`, `chronology.ingested_at`, `chronology.source_timestamp`, `sensitivity.classification`, `sensitivity.contains_customer_data`, `sensitivity.contains_market_data`, `disclosure.policy`, `disclosure.verifier_equivalent`, `disclosure.reviewed_export_approved`, `approval_state.state` | proof package `:547-548,1020-1032` | **Projection**, `CommittedSafe`: closed codes, flags and times the package checks | none | unchanged |
| `business_ids.workflow_id`, `.account_id`, `.desk_id`, `.strategy_id` | proof package `:205-256,376-407` (equality across records) | **Commitment** per field `metadata.mercury.<field>` | the proof package carries each value with a salt-only opening (OQ9 carrier: the package holds the preimage); the verifier opens before comparing | `OpeningUnavailable` or `MetadataCommitmentMismatch` refuses the package |
| every other member: remaining `business_ids`, `chronology.event_id`, `.idempotency_key`, `.causal_parent_event_ids`, `decision_context.workflow_version`, `.policy_reference`, `.model_id`, all `provenance` members, `sensitivity.retention_class`, `disclosure.redaction_profile`, `.audience`, `approval_state.approver_subjects`, `.approval_ticket_id`, `bundle_refs` | proof package `:1020-1032` (event id) and the package display | **Commitment** per field | as above | as above |

**`authorizationContext`, `guardOutcome`, `deniedAccessRecord`, `policySnapshot`** (writer `chio-wall/commands.rs:401-409`, structs in `chio-wall-core/control_path.rs`; no receipt reader). Every member of the four blocks is committed with `salt_ref` `metadata.wall_<block>.<field>`; the wall's `content_hash` over the metadata is replaced by the content commitment. The receipt's top-level `policy_hash` is today `sha256(canonical(policySnapshot))` (`commands.rs:409`), an unsalted digest over committed fields; F sets it to the value of the commitment `metadata.wall_policy_snapshot.policy_hash` over that raw digest, as the cage projection does for its `policy_hash`, so it confirms no guess. The top-level `schema` member (`chio.wall.receipt_metadata.v1`, `commands.rs:402`) is unregistered in P-d and has no reader; F removes it, because the signed `tool_server` (`chio-wall`) names the producer.

| Field | Reader | Form | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `request_id`, `workflow_id`, `actor_label`, `tool_name`, `policy_reference`, `guard_name`, `pipeline_name`, `matched_policy`, `evaluated_tool`, `allowed_tools`, `reason`, `escalation_owner`, `support_owner`, `note`, and `policySnapshot`'s `policy_id`, `allowed_tools` and `note` | none | **Commitment** | operator or caller values |
| `schema`, `buyer_motion`, `control_surface`, `source_domain`, `requested_domain`, `decision`, `fail_closed`, and `policySnapshot`'s `schema`, `source_domain` and `fail_closed` | none | **Commitment** | closed codes with no reader (OQ-A1-1) |

**`acp`** (writer `acp-proxy/kernel_signer.rs:511`): `sessionId` stays in the projection (readers `session_certificate_read.rs:408`, `acp-proxy/compliance.rs:350`; kept only as an entropy-backed id under the id-entropy test, else committed). `authorizationResource` and `authorizationParameterHash` share the commitments of their `receipt_context` aliases (rows above). Every other member (`toolCallId`, `capabilityId`, `authorizationReceiptId`, `authorizationRequestId`, `authorizationToolCallId`, `authorizationCorrelationId`, `authorizationOperation`, `enforcementMode`) has no receipt reader and is a **commitment** `metadata.acp.<field>` (OQ-A1-1), shared with its `receipt_context` alias where the ACP-Client form carries one.

**`active_defense_backfill_v1`** (reader `active_defense_migration.rs:22,1068`): carries the `active_defense` projection (Task 5), never the body; the reader verifies the projection.

**`batch_witness`** (reader `explain.rs:66,776`, a display): **committed** until P-d part 2's audit shows the witness reference is an entropy-backed or keyed Chio checkpoint reference; the audit may then flip it to a projection with `explain.rs` as its named reader.

**Child request receipts.** `ChildRequestReceiptBody.outcome_hash` (`receipt/lineage.rs:35`) is an unsalted SHA-256 over the nested request's outcome payload (`receipt_building.rs:13`), so it confirms a guessed child result. F replaces it with a salted outcome commitment (`salt_ref` `outcome`, the content commitment shape) drawn from the signing process's `Rng`. Its readers are store-only: `receipt_query_snapshot/project.rs:173`, `store_impl.rs:465` and the dedupe comparison at `evidence_retention.rs:1127`. They compare commitment values, which are equal for byte-identical receipts. The digest census found this row; the main plan's producer inventory gains it. The child body's `metadata` (`child_receipt_metadata`, `receipt_building.rs:45-75`) copies the bounded raw error `message`, an unsalted `message_sha256` over the full message, and `outcome`, `message_truncated`, `encoding_error_code`, `original_payload_omitted` and `execution_outcome`. No production code reads any of them, so every member is a **commitment** `metadata.child.<member>` (OQ-A1-1), drawn with the outcome salt from the same `Rng`. Like the outcome commitment, these openings are not persisted (a child receipt id is not a 64-hex receipt id and has no `receipt_openings` rows); the plaintext error reaches the caller's error response and the kernel's structured log. The child body's `terminal_state` reasons (`Cancelled { reason }`, `Incomplete { reason }`, bounded error text from `child_terminal_state`) follow the closed-template rule under "Guard error text and deny reasons". Test `child_receipt_carries_no_error_text`: an errored nested request with a marker in its error text yields a child receipt in which neither the marker nor its SHA-256 appears.

**`keyEnterpriseReceipt`** (writer `chio-control-plane/src/keyring_runtime.rs:1138`; registered `Unsafe` by P-d). The block is the whole signed key enterprise receipt, which is also the keyring receipt's content preimage (`content_hash` over its canonical bytes). It duplicates that preimage, so F removes the block and the content commitment covers it (no new metadata field), as for `finding_pool_mutation`. No production code reads it; the secret-broker process-boundary fault injector (`native_keyring_recovery.rs:26`, a test) selects the active-stage receipt by its receipt id instead of the metadata pointer.

**Sidecar job receipt submission** (writer `chio-api-protect/src/proxy/sidecar.rs:1353-1410`, `sidecar_submit_receipt_metadata`, signed at `sidecar.rs:449`). The submission receipt carries top-level keys copied from the submitted job receipt body: `job_name`, `namespace`, `job_uid`, `outcome`, `started_at`, `completed_at` and `steps` (each `pod_name`, `phase`, `payload`, `observed_at`), plus the constant `submission_kind: "job_receipt"` and the HTTP status scope keys of `http_status_metadata_final`. P-d registers none of the job keys and no production code reads them. P-d registers the HTTP status scope keys (`chio_http_status_scope`, and `chio_decision_receipt_id` on a final receipt) as `ChioInternal` `Unsafe` (reserved; no production reader, OQ-A1-1) until F commits them with the HTTP rows. Every copied value is part of the submitted body, which is the receipt's content preimage, so F removes these keys and the content commitment over the raw body covers them; `submission_kind` is removed because the signed `policy_hash` (`manual_receipt_policy_hash("chio_api_protect_sidecar_receipt_submission")`) already names the submission path. The HTTP status scope keys follow the HTTP rows (Task 12). Test `sidecar_submission_receipt_has_no_job_fields`.

**`chio_http_receipt_v1`, `delivery_contract`, `active_defense_evidence_id`**: already planned (deleted with `HttpReceipt`, Task 12; split, OQ12; keyed, r5).

### Cage projection (Root-2 decision 3)

Every alias of an argument-derived digest is committed, in every location, and the receipt's top-level `policy_hash` follows the projection, so no raw digest survives under another key. The projection binds to the raw cage evidence through one commitment over the whole raw body.

Opening source (one strategy for every cage commitment). The cage receipt is appended to the host's receipt store (`chio-mcp-adapter` `persist_signed_cage_receipt_with_trusted_key` over `receipt_store.append_chio_receipt`), and that append takes the `SignedReceipt`, so every `metadata.cage_receipt.*` entry of `SignedReceipt.openings.metadata` (`MetadataOpening { salt, value }`, Task 6) becomes a `receipt_openings` salt row and a `receipt_metadata_values` value row in the append transaction, replicated and erased with the receipt like any other opening. "The evidence store" in the table below is that receipt store: an operator reader opens with its own rows, a remote reader through `POST /v1/internal/receipts/openings/read` with `receipt-openings:read` (Task 4). No separate cage disclosure file or salt copy exists. The native launch evidence that `chio run` and `chio mcp` export carries salt-only openings only for the two commitments `cage_policy/evidence.rs` checks, whose preimages it holds (Task 16 carrier table).

| Field (all locations) | Producer | Readers | Projection or commitment | Opening | Refusal |

| `plan_digest` in `bindings` and `enforcement_record.fully_enforced.prepared` | `chio-cage/src/launch/linux.rs:216,258-260,1035`, `receipt.rs` (`prepare_cage_receipt`) | cage verifier (`verify_signed_cage_receipt`), launch checks `linux.rs:364-366,676-677,1093-1095` (in-process, raw plan) | **Commitment** `metadata.cage_receipt.plan_digest` (preimage covers `target_argv`, environment, descriptor paths); one commitment, referenced from both locations | in-process launch checks use the raw plan; an authorized verifier opens with the evidence store | `MetadataCommitmentMismatch` |
| `fd_table_digest` in `bindings` and `fully_enforced.prepared` | as above | as above | **Commitment** `metadata.cage_receipt.fd_table_digest` | as above | as above |
| `profile_digest` in `bindings` and `fully_enforced.prepared`, and the receipt's top-level `policy_hash` | as above | cage verifier (`profile_digest == policy_hash`), `cage_policy/evidence.rs` | **Commitment** `metadata.cage_receipt.profile_digest` over the raw profile digest; `policy_hash` is set to that commitment's value, so the verifier's equality still holds and neither confirms a guess | authorized verifier opens with the evidence store | mismatch between `policy_hash` and the commitment refuses |
| `admitted_policy_digest` | `prepare_cage_receipt` | cage verifier; `cage_policy/evidence.rs` (equality with `sha256(signed_policy)`) | **Commitment** when equal to the profile digest (the same commitment value is referenced); otherwise its own commitment | evidence store; `cage_policy/evidence.rs` opens with the salt-only opening the native launch evidence carries | as above |
| `raw_evidence` | cage host | authorized verifier | **Commitment** `metadata.cage_receipt.raw` over the canonical raw `CageReceiptBody`: the verifiable binding to the authoritative raw evidence | an authorized verifier with disclosure authority opens it, then recomputes every field commitment above from the opened raw body | any mismatch refuses |
| `manifest_digest`, `helper_binding_digest`, `target_binding_digest`, `target_identity` | as above | cage verifier and `cage_policy/evidence.rs` (tool and binary identity) | **Projection**, `CommittedSafe`: digests and file identity of operator-installed binaries and the signed manifest, not caller values | none | unchanged |
| `seccomp_filter_digest`, `trace_session_digest`, `applied_execution_identity` | as above | cage verifier (enforcement checks); `cage_policy/evidence.rs` (`applied_execution_identity` equality with the signed policy's runtime execution identity) | **Commitment** per field until the cage producer audit (before F) shows the preimage holds no caller value; the audit may then flip a field to a projection with the verifier as its named reader | authorized verifier opens with the evidence store; `cage_policy/evidence.rs` opens `applied_execution_identity` with the carried salt-only opening | `MetadataCommitmentMismatch` |

### Guard error text and deny reasons (lead #1727 review P1-2)

A guard `Err(e)` becomes `GuardDenied("guard \"{name}\" error (fail-closed): {e}")` (`kernel/dispatch.rs:416-455`, also `dispatch/runtime_admission.rs:156`), which is the signed `Decision::Deny.reason`. The portable kernel builds the same text in `chio-kernel-core/src/evaluate.rs:199-205` (`KernelCoreError::GuardError { guard, reason }`) and appends `reason` for `ConstraintEvaluation`. Example: `HttpProjectionGuard`, declared SAFE, returns `projected.capability.invalid_reason` and serde errors over `ctx.request.arguments` (`chio-http-core/src/authority.rs:249-274`). `evidence_declaration` covers only `GuardEvidence.details`.

- **Signed reason.** `Decision::Deny.reason` and `Decision::Cancelled.reason` become a closed template: `guard "<guard name>" <GuardFailureCode>` for guard errors and `<RefusalCode>` for kernel refusals; the guard name is Chio-defined. No `e` text enters a signed field.
- **Detail.** Every free-text source the template removes keeps its text in a **commitment** with its own `salt_ref` and the plaintext in `receipt_metadata_values` for the operator; an operator reader that needs it reads the values table. Guard `Err` text is `metadata.guard_error.detail`. Dynamic kernel refusal text (a refusal reason built with `format!` around an error, e.g. `allow_responses.rs:55` "capability authorization changed before allow finalization: {error}") is `metadata.refusal.detail`. Host-supplied cancellation text (`session_ops.rs:709` `request_session_cancellation_with_reason`, and the `Cancelled` reasons written at `effect_obligation.rs:470`, `effect_obligation/preparation.rs:172` and `admission_coordinator/refusal.rs:264`) is `metadata.cancellation.detail`. A refusal whose whole reason is the closed code carries no detail field. `guard_error`, `refusal` and `cancellation` are registered `ChioInternal` keys whose only members are commitments (P-d part 2). Readers that copy a reason (`effect_obligation/preparation.rs:217`, `refusal.rs:534`) copy the closed code.
- **SAFE classification rule.** `EvidenceDisclosure::Safe` now requires that the guard's `details`, its deny reason and its `Err` text are all free of argument- or result-derived text. P-d part 2 reclassifies `HttpProjectionGuard` and any other guard whose `Err` interpolates request data to `UNSAFE` and changes guard deny reasons to `&'static str` or a closed enum where they are SAFE (lead #1727 review P3-4).
- **Tests.** `deny_reason_carries_no_argument_text` (marker property: markers in arguments through every guard `Err` path, including `HttpProjectionGuard`, runtime admission hooks and the portable kernel's `GuardError` and `ConstraintEvaluation`, never appear in `Decision::Deny.reason` or `Cancelled.reason`); `guard_error_detail_committed`; `refusal_detail_committed` (a dynamic kernel refusal keeps its text only in the `metadata.refusal.detail` values row); `cancellation_detail_committed` (a host cancellation reason passed to `request_session_cancellation_with_reason` appears only in the `metadata.cancellation.detail` values row); `safe_guard_err_text_is_static`.

### Registry follow-ups (lead #1727 review P1-1)

- `protocol_refusal` and `admission_operation` are registered `Unsafe` in P-d until F applies the rows above.
- **Registered form and disclosure.** Each registry row carries `form` (`Plaintext`, `Projection`, `Commitments` or `Removed`), its target form in a committed receipt, rendered as the PROTOCOL 6.4 Form column, with the named readers in `reason`. `disclosure` states what the key's writer emits today, so P-d part 2 flips no existing key to `CommittedSafe`; the new projection keys (`active_defense`, `response_simulation`, `security_event`) and the detail keys (`guard_error`, `refusal`, `cancellation`) have no earlier writer and register `CommittedSafe`, while `cage_receipt` stays `Unsafe` because the cage producer writes the raw body under it. Each F commit that changes a writer to its registered form flips that row's `disclosure` and adds the key to `WRITER_EMITS_REGISTERED_FORM` in the same commit; `committed_safe_disclosure_never_outruns_the_writer` refuses a `CommittedSafe` row whose form is `Removed` or whose writer is not listed. Whether `form` stays as the documented target after F is decided at F.
- `lineageReferences`: the top-level fallback (`kernel/mod.rs:869-877`) is deleted in the reserved-key PR; F adds no reader of it.
- The store claim log builds authorization details on read from values rows (derived copies are ephemeral, above); `derived_details_follow_values_erasure` pins that an erased `purpose` or `payment_token` refuses and never yields an empty token.
- **Every producer is covered at the signer.** The registry check runs inside the one portable signer (Task 6 signer order, Task 8), so a direct producer that never passes through kernel assembly (keyring, ACP-Client, OTel, Mercury, Wall, the HTTP authority and sidecar, fixtures) cannot sign an unregistered, caller-origin or `Unsafe` key. Task 8's refusal therefore needs every key a producer writes at F's tip in its decided form: the census adds `keyEnterpriseReceipt`, `policySnapshot` and the sidecar submission keys (rows above), and P-d part 2 audits, member by member, every other key that is registered `Unsafe` and that a production writer names, before F.
- `kept_digests_are_keyed_or_entropy_backed` extends to every `CommittedSafe` registry entry and to kept ids (the id-entropy test), not only the security projections, and to every row `digest_aliases.py` marks kept. P-d part 2 audits the next candidates member by member: `caller_delivery`, `chio_runtime`, `finding_purchase_replay_snapshot`, `finding_recovery_replay_snapshot`, `threshold_approval`. P-d part 1 registers `chio_runtime`, `finding_purchase_replay_snapshot` and `finding_recovery_replay_snapshot` as `ChioInternal` `Unsafe` pending that audit; P-d part 2 records each member's decided form and readers in the per-field table before F switches on the signer refusal.

### Split of P-d

- **Reserved-key fix** (Root-2 decision 1): a separate small PR on current main that only expands the caller-metadata reserved list (12 to 29 names) with exact-refusal tests; it lands independently and does not wait for this amendment.
- **P-d part 1** (#1727): guard declarations and the registry, with the classifications above registered `Unsafe` until F.
- **P-d part 2** (stacked, after P-a, P-b and #1706): `MetadataCommitment`; the `active_defense`, `response_simulation`, `security_event`, `stream`, `acp`, `financial` and `cage_receipt` projections; registry rows flipped to their decided forms with named readers; the guard SAFE rule change; the census script and output; the tests that need no F wiring. The plan's merge order and size table now name P-d part 2 explicitly.

### OQ-A1-1 (resolved by Root-2)

- **Decision (Root-2):** fields with no named plaintext reader are committed, including closed codes and Chio ids: `memory_provenance`, `session_report`, the wall blocks, `otel`, `scimLifecycle` and `protocol_admission.last_error_code`. Root-2's decision covers only no-reader fields. It approves no retained field as such. A field this plan keeps plaintext stays so only on this plan's own justification, which Root-2 reviews row by row: a named reader, a provenance that is a closed code or entropy-backed or keyed, and a binding to the value it stands for. The per-field tables give that justification for each kept field (`receipt_context` kernel-issued ids, `chio_receipt_signing_nonce`, `admission_operation.operation_id` and state, the `acp` session id, the financial projections, the `governed_transaction` projection members (`server_id`, `tool_name`, the approval flag, approver key and approval artifact digest, the runtime-assurance closed codes and the economic accounting projection), the cage identity projection), and the id-entropy test commits any kept id that fails it. The request namespace digest is not a kept field: it is committed (the `receipt_context` row). The governed intent hash is not a kept field: it is committed (above), and it stays an open finding until F lands that change.

### OQ-A1-2 (resolved: P-g)

- **Global `attribution.subject_key`.** Every receipt with enterprise attribution carries `attribution.subject_key`, today an unsalted SHA-256 over the provider scope and the canonical principal (`scim_lifecycle.rs:452-461`, `session_identity.rs:467`), so it confirms a guessed principal. Resolved by Root-2's identity decision and the lead's key-set decision: derived enterprise subject keys become keyed values under the `enterprise-subject` purpose subkey of the one tenant index key set, with the lookup contract in P-g below; real public keys keep their form.

### #1710 settlement readers (atomic with F)

A reader may stay untouched by F only if a test on F's final source proves it already consumes the new shape with unchanged behavior. Every reader that needs adaptation, including the denied retained-payment path, changes in F's own commits. There is no intermediate commit with a broken caller and no compatibility decoder. The list, at #1710 head `cbd953833f`, is the input to the lead's settle-hook lane:

| Reader (file:line at `cbd953833f`) | What it reads | Disposition |

Its fixture at `:778-781` uses error code `"timeout"`, outside the closed set, and is regenerated with a closed code |

#1710 is an explicit predecessor of F (merge order above), so these changes are F's: F's commits move the readers listed as moving, and no merged state has a reader of the old shape.

### Identity closure: P-g, a blocking F predecessor (Root-2 identity decision)

The SCIM rows above leave outside-block aliases that A1's metadata scope cannot close alone: `derive_enterprise_subject_key` is an unsalted SHA-256 over the provider scope and the canonical principal, and it has two implementations (`chio-control-plane/src/scim_lifecycle.rs:452`, `chio-mcp-remote/src/remote_mcp/session_identity.rs:467`) whose output feeds capability and identity records, `attribution.subject_key`, SCIM `subjectKey`, the SCIM receipt id, `capability_id` and `policy_hash`. Closing it is **P-g, a named precursor that blocks F**, not a Safe exception.

**Activation boundary.** P-g is byte-neutral, like every other precursor: it adds the derivation, the string form and its parser, the lookup index and its tests, and changes no signed byte and no fixture. F switches every producer and consumer of derived enterprise subject identifiers, together with the SCIM receipt id, `capability_id` and `policy_hash`, in its own commits, and regenerates the affected fixtures with the rest of F's fixtures (D17 covers them). No merged state mixes the unkeyed and keyed forms, and nothing decodes the old 64-hex unkeyed shape after F.

- **Scope.** Derived enterprise subject identifiers only. Real public keys (an agent's Ed25519 `subject_key`, read by signature and authorization readers such as `reputation.rs`, `passport_handlers.rs:1480,1647`, `capital_and_liability.rs:602,845-873`) are entropy-backed and keep their form. P-g's census script classifies every `subject_key` reader and writer as real-key or derived-enterprise, failing closed on an unclassified line.
- **Derivation.** `esk = TenantIndexKeySet::derive(EnterpriseSubject, "chio.enterprise-subject.v1" || 0x00 || len32(provider_scope) || provider_scope || len32(canonical_principal) || canonical_principal)`, carried as the string form `esk1.<hex key_id>.<hex value>` (purpose subkeys, Task 5). The preimage is length-prefixed, so no two (scope, principal) pairs share an input. One function in `chio-core-types` replaces both implementations; the control plane and the remote MCP edge change in the same PR, with no unsalted fallback and no dual decoder.
- **Key.** No separate key set: P-g uses the deployment's one tenant index key set (`--tenant-index-keys <dir>`, Task 5) under the `enterprise-subject` purpose subkey, with its `key_id`, rotation and retired-generation verification. Every process that derives or matches enterprise subjects loads that set, which adds the remote MCP edge (`chio-mcp-remote`) and the control plane's SCIM and federation services to its provisioning list. A value whose `key_id` is not loaded refuses (`TenantIndexKeyUnknown`), and startup refuses a missing set when enterprise federation or SCIM is configured. Cross-node matching compares `(key_id, value)`; every node loads the same generations.
- **SCIM storage.** The SCIM registry is a JSON file today, so it has no unique index and no transaction. P-g gives it concrete storage for the identity index and the transactional re-key: a store table with the unique `(provider_scope, principal)` index and the history table, written in one transaction, or an equivalent single-writer file protocol with an atomic replace that enforces the same uniqueness. The choice respects the held-store and schema-adoption ordering in the merge order above, and P-g names its schema version and the store files it touches before its PR opens.
- **Lookup across rotation (Root-2 #1728 r4 item 2).** The SCIM lifecycle store and the federation identity store hold, per identity, a stable `identity_id` drawn from the `Rng` seam, the provider scope and the canonical principal (operator-private, as today), and the current keyed value `(key_id, value)`. A prior `(key_id, value)` pair is kept in an identity history table, so receipts signed under an older generation still join to the identity.
  - **Resolve.** Fresh logins, SCIM create, update and delete, `find_by_identity` and `bind_capability` (`scim_lifecycle.rs:189-232`) resolve a principal by `(provider scope, canonical principal)` to its `identity_id`, never by comparing derived strings. A remote login presents a value derived under the active generation; the control plane verifies it with `verify_value` against the stored principal, under the generation the value names, and resolves the `identity_id`.
  - **Re-key on rotation.** When the active generation changes, each node re-derives the stored current value of every identity from its private principal under the new active key, in one transaction per store, and moves the old pair to history. Until that transaction commits the store serves no identity reads. Attribution joins map any `(key_id, value)` in a receipt through the current value or history to the `identity_id`.
  - **Deactivate and revoke.** SCIM deactivation and capability revocation records key on `identity_id`, so a rotation neither revives a deactivated identity nor detaches its revocations.
  - **No duplicates.** `identity_id` is unique per `(provider scope, canonical principal)` (a unique index). Create resolves first and inserts only on a miss, in the same write transaction, so a principal never gets a second identity across a rotation.
  - **Unavailable generation.** A stored current value or history row whose `key_id` is not loaded refuses startup of the store (`TenantIndexKeyUnknown`); retired generations stay loaded while history references them. A presented value under an unknown `key_id` refuses. There is no unkeyed fallback.
  - **Tests.** `rotate_then_login_resolves_same_identity`, `scim_deactivate_after_rotation_holds`, `no_duplicate_identity_across_rotation`, `history_join_after_rotation`, `unknown_generation_refuses_startup_and_lookup`, `old_unkeyed_shape_refuses`.
- **SCIM aliases.** The receipt id is drawn from the `Rng` seam. Any index that looked a SCIM receipt up by its derived id is updated to the stored id, never dropped. `capability_id` becomes `"scim-user:"` followed by the string form of `derive(EnterpriseSubject, "chio.scim-capability.v1" || 0x00 || len32(provider_id) || provider_id || len32(user_id) || user_id)`. `policy_hash` is the hex value of `derive(EnterpriseSubject, "chio.scim-policy.v1" || 0x00 || len32(provider_id) || provider_id || len32(subject) || subject)`, whose `key_id` is recorded in the SCIM lifecycle row; a policy hash whose generation is unknown refuses. The block members stay committed per the SCIM table.
- **Evidence (tests).** A dictionary of controlled principals cannot reconstruct a retained identifier without the key. Distinct provider or deployment scopes do not alias. Every control-plane, remote and SCIM equality reader stays correct. A missing or wrong key refuses. The old 64-hex unkeyed shape refuses structurally. No raw principal or unkeyed digest appears in signed artifacts or log exports. The rotation tests above pass.
- **Run.** The Verification matrix for every PR, plus `cs cargo test -p chio-core-types -p chio-control-plane -p chio-mcp-remote --no-fail-fast`, P-g's `subject_key` census script with no unclassified line, and `cs cargo xtask gen signed-fixtures --check` (no change, P-g is byte-neutral).
- **Size.** P-g 3-4 engineer-days (census and classification, the shared derivation and parser, the lookup index, identity history and re-key, provisioning the set to the remote edge and SCIM services, tests); F +1-2 for the atomic switch of both producers, every consumer and the SCIM aliases, with fixture regeneration.

### Tasks

- **P-c:** `receipt_metadata_values` with the 13 probe cases as named Rust tests; included in erasure, replication and snapshot import.
- **P-d part 2:** as above, plus `metadata_salt_refs_fit_64_bytes` in `chio-core-types`: it enumerates every metadata `salt_ref` the tables register, expanding each template over its member set (`cc_<member>` over `GovernedCallChainProvenance`, `metered_<leaf>`, `metadata.otel.attr_<n>` up to the exporter's attribute limit, the Mercury, SCIM, wall, child and ACP-Client members), and asserts each is at most 64 bytes and passes the `receipt_metadata_values.salt_ref` check.
- **P-g:** the byte-neutral enterprise subject derivation, parser, lookup index, identity history and re-key (above); F depends on it.
- **F:** writers emit commitments and values rows in the append transaction; readers move to projections, values rows or openings as the tables say; the #1705 and #1710 settlement readers listed above move to `FinancialSettlementProjection` in F's own commits; the claim log stops persisting derived plaintext; the intent-hash commitment and its carriers; the token fallback deletion; the switch of every derived enterprise subject identifier and the SCIM keyed ids, with fixture regeneration; the deny reason template in both kernels; Task 8's refusal switches on only after every row here has its decided form.
- **F acceptance** (Root-2): exact refusals for `Unsafe`, unknown and caller-origin keys; unchanged behavior of every valid reader in the tables; `ambiguous_denied_receipt_yields_retained_payment`, `payment_token_never_copied_to_plaintext_key`, `intent_hash_has_no_public_alias`, `derived_details_follow_values_erasure`, `charged_receipt_with_accounting_fields_yields_charged`, `financial_block_with_unknown_member_refuses`, `governed_receipt_has_no_plaintext_caller_values` (markers in `max_amount`, seller, settlement destination, metered quote, autonomy and runtime attestation never appear raw or as an unsalted digest in the signed receipt), `child_receipt_carries_no_error_text`, `refusal_detail_committed`, `cancellation_detail_committed`, `sidecar_submission_receipt_has_no_job_fields`, `stream_projection_has_no_chunks_expected`, `same_tenant_receipts_carry_distinct_namespace_commitments`, `recovered_receipt_reuses_persisted_namespace_salt`, the live-parent anchor positive control and the persisted-parent refusal; wrong and missing opening failures for every committed field with a reader; field-level disclosure tests (marker values in every committed field and in deny reasons never appear in signed fields, OCSF or logs); producer-regenerated signed fixtures on the integrated base. All shapes stay v1; no legacy decoder or compatibility path.

### Size

+9 to +13 engineer-days: P-d part 2 +3-4 (commitment type, projections, registry flips, SAFE rule, digest census rules), P-c +1 (values table, ephemeral claim-log details), F +5-8 (writers, readers, the identity switch, settlement enum and retained path, intent-hash commitment and its carriers, token fallback, SCIM keyed ids, deny template in both kernels, tests). P-g adds 3-4 engineer-days as its own precursor and F 1-2 more for the identity switch. Item 2 becomes **L, 68-95 engineer-days, planning figure 80** (including the Task 12 sidecar body and Tower response work); the M1 total becomes 154-220.

## Open Questions

Each needs an owner decision. The plan is written against the recommended default.

1. **`Rng` home and fallibility.** **Default:** move to `chio_core_types::rng` with `fill_bytes -> Result<(), RngError>`; delete `NullRng` and the browser and mobile zero-fill fallbacks; P-a.
2. **Opening persistence versus spec 8.4.** Spec 8.4 says a disclosure-store write failure after signing appends the receipt and marks the opening lost. **Default:** openings are written in the receipt's append transaction (SQLite and trust-control), so an opening is never lost apart from its receipt and a failure is a receipt append failure handled as today. Amend spec 8.4 and Q-B5 to say so in F's PROTOCOL commit.
3. **When the kernel fills `authority`.** **Default:** F adds the type, the `tenant_id` refusal, the schema and the vectors; every producer sets `authority: None`; item 3 fills it.
4. **`receipt_disclosure` verdict-matrix category.** **Default:** stays staged; F lands the kernel tests behind 001 to 006 and the error code.
5. **Metadata policy.** **Default:** drop guard evidence declared `Unsafe` and every caller or hook metadata key; refuse signing on an unregistered or `Unsafe` Chio-internal key; register every internal key in the ADR-0019 item 7 registry in P-d.
6. **Security bodies in signed metadata.** **Default:** option (a) of Root-2's r2 request 2: `active_defense_body` and `response_simulation_report` leave signed metadata; each is replaced by an audited projection that commits the raw tenant and plan identifiers (Task 5), and `cage_receipt` follows its audit. No privacy-contract change is needed. The alternative, option (b), keeps the full bodies as public Chio-internal metadata, which requires amending spec 7.6 and the fully-committed claim; this plan does not take it.
7. **HTTP body preimage.** **Default:** always the raw bytes, tagged: `{"kind":"bytes","content_type":..,"b64u":..}` or `{"kind":"absent"}`, never parsed; a body refused at a size limit (or a refused event stream) is `{"kind":"refused","limit":..}` and a response body whose read failed or missed its collection deadline is `{"kind":"read_failed","bytes_read":..}`, the guard naming which (Task 12, Tower response body: the deadline is the remaining request budget, or 30 s when no request timeout is set); the sidecar receives the body bytes in its evaluate request (G3), bounded by `input::MAX_BODY_BYTES`; vectors pin the lookalike pair.
8. **Offline nonce parameter binding in `authoritative_spend`.** The offline check compares the nonce's bound parameter digest with the receipt's `parameter_hash`, which F removes; the spend receipt differs from the allow receipt the nonce was minted from, so no commitment equality can replace it. **Default:** the parameter binding is checked at signing time by the kernel (`reconciliation.rs`), and the offline check reduces to the signed `budget_authority.execution_nonce_id` equal to the nonce id. This default holds only if PROTOCOL drops the offline parameter-binding claim for authoritative spend; F's PROTOCOL commit drops it.
9. **Salt-only openings in carriers.** Spec 7.7 lists only the caller copy, `caller` bundles and the PEP response. **Default:** amend spec 7.7 with: "A salt-only opening for the `<salt_ref>` commitment may accompany an artifact that carries that commitment's complete preimage. It is never written for a field whose preimage the artifact does not carry, and erasure does not reach openings already delivered in such artifacts." It applies only to the Task 16 carriers, each for its named `salt_ref`: commerce authority receipt artifacts, settle and web3 anchor proofs, enterprise export events, agent-web envelopes, Mercury and attest-loopback package steps, settle finding-enforcement proofs, finding challenge replay evidence, finding bundle producing receipts (the three runtime-assurance commitments the finding verifier's runtime-assurance facet checks against the attestation it verified), the four credit carriers of an obligation atom (channel advance, credit admission record, factor claim, IOU; the payee binding commitment, whose raw digest each rebuilds from the creditor and destination it holds in plaintext), and native launch evidence (`NativeLaunchEvidence`, `NativeLaunchObservation`, for the two cage commitments `cage_policy/evidence.rs` checks). It does not apply to the finding verifier's delivery check, which holds only `payload_sha256` and binds to `delivery_contract` (kind F). The alternative, rebinding each carrier consumer to a receipt id recorded by a signed Chio artifact at signing time, adds about 3 days.
10. **`/v1/evaluate` openings.** **Default:** none in M1 (spec 7.7 and the 13.1 caller-copy cut); mediated mode persists them, sidecar mode without a store drops them.
11. **Signed tenant binding beyond the security producers.** The keyed `tenant_binding` member is an addition to spec 7.6, which today allows only a plaintext `tenant_id` or, with `authority`, no signed tenant at all. **Default:** add it to spec 7.6 in F's PROTOCOL commit for the native evidence, response simulation and cage producers only; every other producer keeps `tenant_id` until a later item decides whether all tenant-scoped receipts move to the binding.
12. **Plaintext `delivery_contract` digests for finding-delivery grants.** The kernel decides that a grant is a finding-delivery grant from the grant's signed constraint set (a `FindingDelivery` constraint on the capability), never from an argument or caller-supplied marker, so a caller cannot select the plaintext form. **Default:** keep them plaintext, because they equal the finding's `payload_sha256`, which the listing publishes, and the finding verifier holds only that digest; every other `OutputDigestSha256` grant's `observed_digest` becomes a salted commitment. The entropy argument is the listing's: no new value is disclosed. The alternative commits the finding-delivery digests too and moves the finding verifier to an opening the listing would have to carry.

## Unresolved in this revision

- The store schema-adoption cleanup has no PR or implementation yet. It is the last serial store item and an explicit unresolved predecessor of P-c and F; this plan names no PR number for it.
- #1722 is a draft at `188a6af6ff`; it is not compiled or runtime-green yet, and its native stage 1 and 2 follow C6.
- Open questions 2 (openings in the append transaction versus spec 8.4), 8 and 9 need owner decisions and PROTOCOL amendments. If 9 is rejected, Task 16 switches the carrier consumers to the rebind alternative and the size rises by about 3 days.
- P-e's owner is open: it may land from the fwdcompat lane or from this lane; it precedes F either way.
- Open question 11 (the signed tenant binding as a spec 7.6 addition) needs an owner decision.
- Open question 12 (plaintext finding-delivery `delivery_contract` digests) needs an owner decision.
- P-f changes every deployment's trust-control configuration (caller credentials replace `service_token` and `tenant_read_tokens`; internal routes move to a second listener); the mapping of today's routes to scopes is fixed in P-f's PR and reviewed by Root-2 before it opens.
- P-e is lead-owned in the fwdcompat lane.
- The per-peer cluster credential (Ed25519 key per node) is new cluster configuration; operators of existing clusters provision it before F.
- The P-d cage audit (`crates/security/chio-cage/tests/cage_receipt_disclosure_audit.rs`, #1727) finds `cage_receipt` not committed-safe (`plan_digest` and `fd_table_digest` hash the target argv, environment and descriptor paths), so the cage gets the A1 projection, with openings persisted by the cage receipt's append and carried by the native launch evidence (Tasks 10 and 16).
